# Shopify

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Supported channels
> 
>   * LINE
>   * Messenger
> 


Connect your Shopify store to the BotBonnie chatbot so users can view their shopping information, receive notifications for orders and restocked products. With this integration, users who linked their accounts can view their [transaction history](doc:e-commerce-features-query-transaction-history) and receive [automatic status notifications](doc:e-commerce-features-automatic-status-notifications).

Follow these setup steps to add a connection:

  1. Create an API token
  2. Connect with Shopify
  3. Set up account linking
  4. (Optional) Set up notifications



After setup, you can [manage connection](doc:e-commerce-integrations#manage-connections) whenever necessary.

* * *

## 

1\. Create an API token

Before connecting BotBonnie to your Shopify store, you'll need to create a custom app in Shopify console and generate an API token. This token allows BotBonnie to securely access your store's data.

  1. Follow [Shopify's guide](https://help.shopify.com/en/manual/apps/app-types/custom-apps) to create a custom app.
  2. When configuring Admin API scopes, enable these required permissions:
     * `read_orders`
     * `write_customers`
     * `read_customers`
     * `read_products`
     * `read_custom_fulfillment_services`
     * `read_fulfillments`
     * `read_merchant_managed_fulfillment_orders`
     * `read_third_party_fulfillment_orders`
     * `read_returns`
     * `read_shipping`



> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> Copy and save the Admin API access token immediately after installing your as it's only displayed once for security reasons.

* * *

## 

2\. Connect with Shopify

  1. In your BotBonnie console, open the project launcher dropdown in the top navigation bar, select **Common settings** , and then click **EC platforms** in the left navigation panel.

![](https://files.readme.io/42d9e57b124e8eaa22c136bae1bc157fed1c1f4b734c857cb4a8a5c4c63055d4-nav-ec-platforms.png)

  2. Click the **Shopify** card to start the connection.

![](https://files.readme.io/6f5a00332395029765e70afd4445d321beb3f6bfeeb61334b01ee4e37bf4f073-add-connection-shopify.png)

  3. Enter connection name and click **\+ Choose page(s)** to select the Messenger or LINE Official Account you want to connect.

![](https://files.readme.io/ee056ef74f4148c653c09b0d94efbd6d9dbbd749641263a7109675e36ee85916-shopify-pages.png)

  4. Enter the following Shopify account details:
     1. Shop ID: Your Shopify store identifier.
     2. API Token: The token you created in step 1.

![](https://files.readme.io/0acf58ddf0055c5d324b8e3f4b8f09b040231ff5fd922c825698bfd751ea872b-shopify-account-details.png)

  5. Click **Next** to continue to set up account linking.



* * *

## 

3\. Set up account linking

  1. Under **User login method** , select how users authenticate: **Phone number only** , **Email only** , or **Either phone number or email**.
  2. (Optional) Check the **Apply monthly SMS limit** and specify the maximum number of SMS verification messages per month to control your budget.
  3. Enter a **Redirect URL** , the webpage where users will register or log into their accounts to complete [account linking](doc:e-commerce-integration-account-linking).

![](https://files.readme.io/1d05170a1722f55494e2771791584ef11995308c00f0f5397345abe6c7f4f7a3-shopify-redirect-url.png)

  4. Customize the **Terms and conditions** text to display during the account linking flow.
  5. (Optional) Configure the following options:
     * **Message of successful linking** : Enable to send success message when account linking succeeds.
     * **Switch menu group or triggered actions after account linking** : Activate specific [trigger action](doc:triggered-actions) after successful linking.

![](https://files.readme.io/4dc824c5f30428586a566335a91594211af5103729b22681760ec93f24b854d1-account-linked-actions.png)

  6. Click **Next** in to continue to set up notifications.



* * *

## 

4\. (Optional) Set up notifications

Enable automatic notifications to alert users about order and stock updates to enhance user experience. For detailed configuration options, see [Automatic status notifications](doc:e-commerce-features-automatic-status-notifications).

__Updated 7 months ago

# SHOPLINE

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Supported channels
> 
>   * LINE
>   * Messenger
> 


Connect your SHOPLINE store to the BotBonnie chatbot so users can view their shopping information, receive notifications and interact with digital member card through supported messaging channels. With this integration, users who link their accounts can:

  * View their [transaction history](doc:e-commerce-features-query-transaction-history)
  * Receive [automatic status notifications](doc:e-commerce-features-automatic-status-notifications)
  * Interact with their digital [member card](doc:e-commerce-features-member-card) (LINE only)



Follow these setup steps to add a connection:

  1. Connect with SHOPLINE
  2. Set up account linking
  3. (Optional) Set up notifications
  4. Install BotBonnie in SHOPLINE



After setup, you can [manage connections](doc:e-commerce-integrations#manage-connections) on whenever necessary.

* * *

## 

1\. Connect with SHOPLINE

Follow these steps to connect your SHOPLINE store to BotBonnie.

  1. Open the project launcher dropdown in the top navigation bar, select **Common settings** , and then click **EC platforms** in the left navigation panel.

![](https://files.readme.io/42d9e57b124e8eaa22c136bae1bc157fed1c1f4b734c857cb4a8a5c4c63055d4-nav-ec-platforms.png)

  2. Click the **SHOPLINE** card to start the connection.

![](https://files.readme.io/81a32928519db9eec203ba92b53aa8aea5fcffd1b611d03120ac0d202453e6f9-shopline-integration.png)

  3. Enter a name for your connection. Then click **\+ Choose page(s)** to select the Facebook Messenger or LINE Official Account you want to connect.

![](https://files.readme.io/d718bee2167470b51512dca0454c65a6cb1de41f50d9e9e642aa155c88a6bc49-choose-page-to-connect.png)

  4. Enter your **SHOPLINE merchant ID**. To get your merchant ID:
     1. Go to your SHOPLINE store homepage.
     2. Copy the subdomain (the part before `.shoplineapp.com`) from your store URL.

![](https://files.readme.io/ac2e309096267db7cd2d880b326207d58b21f7342d8b6676151e26074de1694f-shopline-merchant-id.png)

  5. When you're done, select **Next** in the upper-right corner to continue to set up account linking.



* * *

## 

2\. Set up account linking

  1. Under **User login method** , select how users authenticate: **Phone number only** , **Email only** , or **Either phone number or email**.
  2. (Optional) Check the **Apply monthly SMS limit** box and specify the maximum number of SMS verification messages per month to control your budget.
  3. Enter a **Redirect URL** where users can register as members. This URL is essential for initiating the [account linking process](doc:e-commerce-integration-account-linking#set-up-an-entry-point-for-account-linking) via chatbot flows or menus.

![](https://files.readme.io/22745d4d7473231ba01d3a41c6e89508ac5df7431453d50b88ba345135450c93-redirect-url-shopline-signup.png)

  4. Customize the **Terms and conditions** text to display during the account linking flow.
  5. Configure the following options:
     * **Message of successful linking** : Enable this to send a confirmation message when the system successfully links an account .
     * **Switch menu group or triggered actions after account linking** : Enable to [trigger specific actions](doc:triggered-actions) after successful linking.

![](https://files.readme.io/4dc824c5f30428586a566335a91594211af5103729b22681760ec93f24b854d1-account-linked-actions.png)

  6. When you're done, select **Next** in the upper-right corner to continue to set up notifications.



* * *

## 

3\. (Optional) Set up notifications

Enable automatic notifications to alert users about order, point, reward credit, and coupon updates to enhance user experience. For detailed configuration options, see [Automatic status notifications](doc:e-commerce-features-automatic-status-notifications).

* * *

## 

4\. Install BotBonnie in SHOPLINE

To complete the integration, follow the steps below to install the BotBonnie app in your SHOPLINE store:

  1. Go to [SHOPLINE console](https://sso.shoplineapp.com/users/sign_in), select **Apps** > **Apps Store** on the side navigation bar, then click the BotBonnie card.

![](https://files.readme.io/0e9079ba019950284c84a01a1e68bd5b46ffa578bcf737d9492833205465d6ef-shopline-console.png)

  2. You’ll be redirected to a form. Fill out and submit the form. The customer success manager will reach out to you to complete the installation in SHOPLINE and discuss the optional SMS messaging support.

# E-Commerce Integrations

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Premium feature
> 
> E-commerce integration is a premium feature. Contact your customer success manager for more details.

Integrate your BotBonnie chatbot with leading e-commerce platforms to deliver personalized customer experiences. Once users link their accounts, you can access features like transaction history queries, automated order notifications, membership card displays, and restock alerts to enhance customer engagement and support.

![](https://files.readme.io/67ef273-integration-features.png)

* * *

## 

Getting started

To unlock personalized services for your users, you need to set up two main components:

  1. Connect BotBonnie with your e-commerce platforms to access user details, order data, and product information.
  2. Enable [account linking](doc:e-commerce-integration-account-linking). This essential step connects each user's chat identity to their e-commerce account, enabling users to access [all personalized features](doc:e-commerce-integrations-features) directly through your chatbot.



* * *

## 

Set up your connection

Select your e-commerce platform below to begin the connection process:

[![91APP](https://files.readme.io/0ab055493c5afe5ccb2d5af15379ee7c91337f1df57e00c6a2ec621c08671379-91app.jpg)91APP](https://docs.botbonnie.appier.com/docs/e-commerce-integration-91app)[![Cyberbiz](https://files.readme.io/d166ebd5f27c0b5832117912d5dff3f3faa1e2363cedfcec85f2f406efc635d8-cyberbiz.jpg)Cyberbiz](https://docs.botbonnie.appier.com/docs/e-commerce-integration-cyberbiz)[![Shopify](https://files.readme.io/166f1e2e0b041fcefd96bf2a284888a2dca5c0b444daba86e3a42009cfa65410-Shopify-5x.png)Shopify](https://docs.botbonnie.appier.com/docs/e-commerce-integration-shopify)[![SHOPLINE](https://files.readme.io/891b7201f9b8f77a6113d947e17d6070a662485283f864899489225ef730664d-nameSHOPLINE_stylefullColor_size24.png)SHOPLINE](https://docs.botbonnie.appier.com/docs/e-commerce-integration-shopline)

* * *

## 

Manage connections

After completing the initial setup, you can manage your e-commerce platform connection settings at any time.

To manage your connection, open the project launcher in the top navigation bar, then select **Common settings** > **EC platforms** :

  * Select your connection to modify account info or account linking configuration.
  * Click the trash icon next to your connection to disconnect.

![](https://files.readme.io/6a8dcfd420908a5bd77aa753b4a3376101b22a3f1844d23257be3d52a9290a8b-manage-connection.png)

__Updated 7 months ago

* * *

# Supported Features

After you've connected your [e-commerce platform with BotBonnie](doc:e-commerce-integrations) and your users have [linked their accounts](doc:e-commerce-integration-account-linking), a wide range of features becomes available. These features leverage customer data to provide a more personalized and engaging chat experience.

The following table describes all supported features after a user links their account:

Feature| Description| Supported platform| Supported channel  
---|---|---|---  
[Query transaction history](doc:e-commerce-features-query-transaction-history)| Enables users to view their past order records directly in the chat.| • 91APP   
• Cyberbiz   
• Shopify   
• SHOPLINE| • LINE   
• Messenger  
[Order notifications](doc:e-commerce-features-automatic-status-notifications)| Notifies users about order updates, such as placed, shipped, delivered, or refunded.| • Cyberbiz   
• Shopify   
• SHOPLINE| • LINE   
• Messenger  
[Point notifications](doc:e-commerce-features-automatic-status-notifications)| Notifies users when they earn or spend points.| • Cyberbiz   
• SHOPLINE| • LINE  
[Reward notifications](doc:e-commerce-features-automatic-status-notifications)| Notifies users when reward credits are earned or redeemed.| • SHOPLINE| • LINE  
[Coupon notifications](doc:e-commerce-features-automatic-status-notifications)| Notifies users when they receive new coupons.| • SHOPLINE| • LINE  
[Restock notifications](doc:e-commerce-features-restock-notifications)| Notifies users when out-of-stock items are restocked.| • Shopify| • LINE  
[Member card](doc:e-commerce-features-member-card)| Enables users to check their membership details, such as tier level, points balance, and available benefits.| • Cyberbiz   
• Shopify   
• SHOPLINE| • LINE  
[Product tag syncing](doc:e-commerce-features-product-tag-syncing)| Makes product tags from e-commerce purchases available in user profiles.| • Shopify| N/A  
  
 __Updated 4 months ago

# Entry Trigger Node

The entry trigger node allows you to specify how users can enter the journey. Click **Add a trigger** and select a trigger type:

  * Past condition
  * Real-time event
  * Date-based

![](https://files.readme.io/c55c5cc-Screen_Shot_2024-03-21_at_5.12.33_PM.png)

* * *

## 

Past condition

You can use existing segments to be the past conditions, or manually define the conditions using events and attributes. After the journey becomes active, the system will import users who meet the conditions into the journey. After the initial import, the system will check for qualified users every 6 hours and add them to the journey.

  * **Use segments** : Select segments you want to include or exclude. You need to include at least one segment.
  * **Define conditions** : Select the events and attributes you want to include or exclude.
    * The **Users to include** section allows you to include users who have the specified user attributes or have performed the specified user events. The **Users to exclude** section allows you to exclude users who have the specified user attributes or events.
    * You can add one or multiple conditions. If you select **All** , users need to match all conditions to be considered a match. If you select ​**Any** , users who match any of the conditions will be considered a match.
    * Enter a number for **Only consider events in the last x days**. Only events that occurred within that time period will be used to calculate qualified users. This setting does not apply to user attributes that are set as conditions.



> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Limitation
> 
> New offline users uploaded within one day may not be processed in time to be considered in the past conditions.

![](https://files.readme.io/3abf099-Screen_Shot_2023-11-28_at_1.41.04_PM.png)

* * *

## 

Real-time event

You can specify up to 10 events within the entry node to serve as entry triggers. Users who complete any of the specified events will immediately enter the journey. Note that:

  * Users will only enter the journey if the entry trigger event is completed after the journey becomes active.
  * If the trigger event is an offline event, the event timestamp must be later than the journey activation time, and the users will only enter the journey after the offline events are uploaded.
  * When setting event parameter conditions, the `is not` operator evaluates to `true` if the event doesn't contain the parameter. For example, `"product_category" is not "book"` will evaluate to `true` if the entry trigger event doesn't contain the `product_category` event parameter.

![](https://files.readme.io/fba4131c89b78e4d742ff8bdbed85774903b9263aa8602e04cdb649945941e53-multiple-event.png)

You can also filter journey participants by explicitly including and excluding existing audience segments under **Audience filter**.

  * **Include** : Users in one of the included segments are entered into the journey after they perform the trigger event.
  * **Exclude** : Users who perform the trigger event but are in an excluded segment will not enter the journey.



> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Segment refresh period
> 
> Segments are refreshed every six hours in journey maps, meaning that users may experience a delay of up to six hours before being added or removed from a segment.

![](https://files.readme.io/8a8c10116edf54cdfaa733438a96edd23bf6dd3dfe950a767400b89205e61474-real-time-event-include-exclude-segments.png)

* * *

## 

Date-based

You can let users enter the journey based on a date that is associated with that user. Common use cases for a date-based trigger include:

  * Birthday reward: Send a coupon to users in the month of their birthday.
  * Membership expiration date: Send a renewal reminder to users five days before their membership expires.



> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> See [Tracking date format data](doc:getting-started-with-journey-maps#tracking-date-format-data) for requirements to use user attributes as date-based triggers. Currently, event parameters cannot be used as date-based trigger.

Select the user attribute you want to use as the trigger, and set up the timing of the trigger. If you want to use a date in the past, such as the user's birthday, select **Ignore the year in the date**.

In the screenshot below, users will enter the journey on the first day of the month of their birthday. For example, a user with a birthday on 1980-04-10 will enter the journey on April 1 every year during the journey schedule. If **Ignore the year in the date** is not selected, the journey will not be triggered because the user's birthday is in 1980 when the year is considered.

![](https://files.readme.io/3723216-Screen_Shot_2024-03-22_at_10.27.36_AM.png)

__Updated 5 months ago

* * *

# Export User Data for LINE Ads

BotBonnie allows you to export a list of users in a CSV file and import the users to your LINE Ads account for retargeting. You can either export all LINE users, or export only the LINE users who meet certain filter conditions (e.g. users who have the tag `dessert`). This feature helps you target the right users with relevant content on LINE Ads.

## 

Export LINE users from the BotBonnie console

  1. From the left menu, go to **Audience > User list**.

![](https://files.readme.io/24bfb70-nav-audience-user-list.png)

  2. Click **Filter** , click **Add condition** , set **Platform** to LINE, and select your LINE Official Account.



> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> The LINE Ads account needs to be registered using a LINE account that has administrator access to the LINE Official Account selected here.

![](https://files.readme.io/db86751-user-list-filter-condition.png)

If needed, you can click **Add condition** to add more segmentation conditions. For example, you can choose to include only LINE users with the tag `dessert`.

![](https://files.readme.io/daab5e3-Screen_Shot_2023-03-15_at_3.52.08_PM.png)

  3. On the right, click **Bulk action** and select **Export user data**.

![](https://files.readme.io/79c82d5-Screen_Shot_2023-03-15_at_4.07.36_PM.png)

  4. Type your email address and click **Confirm**.

![](https://files.readme.io/ac80807-Screen_Shot_2023-03-15_at_4.06.43_PM.png)

  5. Go to your inbox and download the file.

![](https://files.readme.io/e544c01-Screen_Shot_2022-09-15_at_3.25.01_PM.png)

  6. Open the CSV file, delete the header row, and save it as a new CSV file.

![](https://files.readme.io/0c1d9c4-Screen_Shot_2022-09-15_at_3.20.37_PM.png)

If you open the CSV file in Excel and the numbers (e.g. User ID, Account ID) are displayed in scientific notation, you can change the data format to **Number**.

![](https://files.readme.io/f30d9bc-Screen_Shot_2022-09-15_at_5.57.56_PM.png)

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
>   * LINE only accepts up to 1,500,000 LINE user IDs in a CSV file.
>   * To use the uploaded user list in LINE Ads, the list needs to include more than 100 valid, reachable LINE user IDs.
> 


* * *

## 

Import LINE users to LINE Ads account

  1. Log in to LINE Official Account Manager and click the account name of your LINE channel.
  2. In the left menu, go to **Data controls > Audiences**, and click **Create New**.

![](https://files.readme.io/df6f808-Screen_Shot_2022-09-15_at_7.50.11_PM.png)

  3. Set Audience type to **User ID upload** , enter an audience name, and click **Next**. You can name the audience based on the purpose of your LINE Ads campaign (e.g. `User ID upload: Christmas sale`).

![](https://files.readme.io/27ed0ed-Screen_Shot_2022-09-15_at_8.01.39_PM.png)

  4. Click **Browse +** , select the CSV file, and click **Update**.

![](https://files.readme.io/80d8e1b-Screen_Shot_2022-09-20_at_2.15.43_PM.png)

The newly created audience will appear in the audience list.

  * It may take a while for LINE to finish processing it.
  * Make sure the **Share status** is set to Public.

![](https://files.readme.io/be1631d-Screen_Shot_2022-09-20_at_2.39.44_PM.png)

You can now go to your LINE Ads account, click the menu icon at the top-left corner of the page, and click **Audience** under Shared library. You will see the audience you uploaded in the audience list.

![](https://files.readme.io/73a3f8c-Screen_Shot_2022-09-20_at_6.45.44_PM.png)

__Updated 11 months ago

# Export User Data for Meta Ads

## 

Overview

BotBonnie enables you to export your bot's user data to CSV files for retargeting campaigns in Meta Ads. You can export all users who connected through Facebook or apply filters to target specific user segments from your BotBonnie audience. This feature helps you retarget your existing bot users with relevant content through Meta Ads campaigns.

### 

Prerequisites

  * Make sure your [Meta Ads account](https://adsmanager.facebook.com/adsmanager/) is linked to a Facebook account with admin access to your Facebook Page.
  * If you've connected more than one Facebook Page to BotBonnie, make sure you're selecting the right Page when you export and upload. This helps ensure Meta can match your audience data accurately.



* * *

## 

1\. Export user data from BotBonnie

  1. In the BotBonnie console, go to **Audience** > **User list** from the left navigation menu.

![](https://files.readme.io/27bbaba1a4f5da0778a48ba890750b95de475a64e2671dcf49f52cfa484d6e0f-user-list.png)

  2. Use the [**Filter**](doc:user-list#filtering-the-user-list) to target users who interacted with your Messenger bot on Facebook. This ensures your exported data is relevant for retargeting.
     * Click **\+ Add condition** , select **Platform** > **Facebook** > the Facebook Page you want to target, then click **Apply** to run the filter.

![](https://files.readme.io/4276b9e0e5c65ccea73c7d7875eea2e55499eeb3c4f013f478729dfbae9336ec-filter-facebook.png)

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> If you've connected multiple Facebook Pages, make sure to select the correct Page in the filter. Meta can only match users who interacted with that Page, and your Meta Ads account must have admin access to it.

  3. Click **Bulk actions** in the upper right, then select **Export user data**.

![](https://files.readme.io/4ad094ef9df876ba5c77a77c39f4401e6c344a5a9d27dcfdf6c403178f463c7f-export-user-data.png)

  4. Enter your email address and check the number of users to be exported before clicking **Confirm**.

![](https://files.readme.io/2a64518aed1a60f0843f606e2f93fd4fbcf3348702cd8e1b2e7cfbb8b5b787d6-export-enter-email.png)

  5. Check your email inbox and download the exported file.

![](https://files.readme.io/f920d520ed73a9b690f41bdb2107e8c9fa52769deb9d210a64c4886d080d2b24-email-download-link.png)

* * *

## 

2\. Format the exported file for Meta Ads

Before uploading your audience list to Meta Ads, prepare your CSV file using the following steps to meet [Meta's audience list requirements](https://www.facebook.com/business/help/2082575038703844).

  * Include at least one main identifier to allow Meta to match the user.
    * We recommend using the `User ID` column from your BotBonnie export and renaming the column header to `pageuid`.
  * Remove unnecessary columns. For example, if you only want to use the `User ID`, keep that column and delete the rest before uploading.
  * Rename column headers to match [Meta's required field names](https://www.facebook.com/business/help/2082575038703844).

![](https://files.readme.io/8c8444f741fb88e200b984da21702f8ceb4d82e31250f85029410e0f4ba409b7-csv-file-to-upload.png)

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Tip
> 
> Including more identifiers may improve Meta's match rate. However, you may need to check and reformat your exported data to meet Meta's formatting rules.

* * *

## 

3\. Import the file into Meta Ads Manager

Once your CSV file is ready, follow the steps in [Meta's guide](https://www.facebook.com/business/help/170456843145568) to create an audience list in Meta Ads Manager.

__Updated 11 months ago

# Facebook

Growth tools help brands engage with users on Facebook through automated interactions on Facebook posts and Messenger conversations. These tools enable brands to:

  * [Auto reply for Post Comments](doc:auto-reply-for-facebook-posts): Automatically reply to comments on Facebook posts.
  * [Auto reply for Messenger Ads (JSON)](doc:auto-reply-for-messenger-ads-json): Send an automated message using Messenger Ads JSON to trigger chatbot flows set up in BotBonnie with a JSON code.

# Flow Insight

## 

Overview

Flow insight is BotBonnie’s interactive hotspot analysis graph which provides a holistic view of conversion rates for conversations between users and chatbots and breakpoints in the conversion process, allowing you to analyze customer needs to improve your chatbot design. You can also drill down into each module to view the number of clicks and click-through rates (CTR) of various buttons, allowing you to optimize the design of your next marketing campaign, one module at a time.

Flow insight presents bot data in a thermal image format, allowing you to adjust the time period, channels, and performance display methods. Deeper shades of color are used to represent the volume of traffic going through each module, letting you intuitively evaluate the effectiveness of your flow by:

  * Understanding customers’ most frequently viewed information
  * Analyze specific customer flows and find breakpoints
  * Optimizing flows through data driven analysis



* * *

## 

How to use Flow insight

From the left menu, go to **Analytics > Flow insight**. In the bottom left corner of the page, there is a legend showing five different colors shades representing the number of users in each module of the flow modules.

![](https://files.readme.io/0e38cc6-01.png)

Next, set the appropriate settings and filters:

  1. Set a time interval
  2. Set up channel filters
  3. Set data display format



### 

1\. Set a time interval

After entering the insights screen, first go to the upper right and select the time interval you want to view. In addition to customizing the time, you can also click on the left to quickly filter by time interval:

![](https://files.readme.io/7857dab-02.png)

### 

2\. Set up channel filters

In the toolbar, click the channel dropdown and select the messaging channels you want to analyze.

![](https://files.readme.io/3e921e2-03.png)

### 

3\. Set data display format

In the toolbar, determine how to display metrics for each node:

  * **Display by count** shows the number of users in that node who completed an action.
  * **Display by percentage** shows the percentage of users in that node who completed an action.

![](https://files.readme.io/4ade43a-04.png)

* * *

## 

Module drilldown

Click on each module to see more detailed analysis data:

  * **Users** : The number of unique users who have interacted with the module
  * **Replies** : The total number of times each message in this module was sent
  * **Module click rate** : Among the users who saw this module, how many unique users interacted with it?
  * **Total number of module clicks** : Click data is only provided for the response module and the Quick Reply of the response module.
    * Note for Quick Reply: Counts only include users who click to go to another module—it doesn't include clicks that send users to another channel or external website.

![](https://files.readme.io/2ad9295-05.png)

__Updated 12 months ago

* * *

[Kit Report](/docs/kit-report)[Live Chat Analytics](/docs/analytics-live-chat)

# Flow

## 

Overview

Your chatbot flow determines the sequence of messages users receive when interacting with the chatbot. A flow consist of a series of connected [modules](doc:modules-and-messages), each of which can contain messages and components to transition users through the flow.

![](https://files.readme.io/80ae008-flow.png)

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Modules and messages
> 
> See [modules and messages](doc:modules-and-messages) to learn more about creating and editing modules and messages within your chatbot flow.

* * *

## 

Viewing and editing your flow

To view or edit your chatbot flow, open the left menu and click the **Flow** icon.

![](https://files.readme.io/82460cf35ef309794441187ed2d173aecb4b7222412566246c2ecbbd46f45765-flow.png)

### 

Reset position in the flow

To reset your position in the flow back to the chatbot's entry point ("Get Started"), click **Reposition** in the toolbar.

![](https://files.readme.io/e093844-flow-reposition.png)

### 

Flow paths

Paths are the connections between modules represented by a colored line, where the color indicates a different type of connection.

Path type| Path color| Example  
---|---|---  
[Button](doc:button)| Gray| ![Button example](https://files.readme.io/ba49135-connection-button.png)  
[Quick reply](doc:quick-reply)| Blue| ![Quick reply example](https://files.readme.io/9c214e1-connection-quick-reply.png)  
[User input](doc:user-input)| Green| ![User input example](https://files.readme.io/5f61cde-connection-user-input.png)  
[Go-to module](doc:go-to-module)| Brown| ![Go-to module example](https://files.readme.io/e48ab8a-connection-go-to-module.png)  
  
* * *

## 

Sub-flows

Sub-flows can be used to group modules into logical components, allowing for a more organized main flow. For example, you can add all modules related to frequently asked questions into a sub-flow named "FAQ":

![](https://files.readme.io/9cded6f-modules-and-sub-flow.png)

  * In your main flow, you'll only see a single "FAQ" sub-flow, reducing clutter.
  * You can freely add and remove modules from the "FAQ" sub-flow.



After you've created and opened a sub-flow, you can add modules, advanced kits, rule kits, and even additional sub-flows just as you would with your main flow.

### 

Adding a sub-flow

To add a sub-flow, click **Sub-flow** in the toolbar.

![](https://files.readme.io/5794006-create-sub-flow.png)

Open a sub-flow to view or edit its contents.

### 

Opening a sub-flow

Sub-flows can be opened in two ways:

  1. From the containing flow, double-click the sub-flow module.
  2. From your main flow or another sub-flow, click on the tab with the sub-flow's name. All of your sub-flows are displayed above the toolbar for quick access.

![](https://files.readme.io/4325f3b-open-sub-flow.png)

### 

Moving modules between flows

To move modules to a sub-flow or main flow, hover over the module you'd like to move and click the three vertical dots that appear in the top-right corner. Hover over **Move to** , then select the destination flow.

![](https://files.readme.io/9990eb5-move-modules.png)

* * *

## 

Testing and deploying flows

### 

Testing a flow

When you're ready to deploy your flow, click **Test** in the flow editor toolbar.

![](https://files.readme.io/518dc89-test-flow.png)

A Messenger window will open and you'll be prompted to log in to your Facebook account. After logging in, you'll be able to interact with your chatbot in the test chatroom.

![](https://files.readme.io/ea92e33-test-flow-example.png)

### 

Deploying a flow

Deploying your flow to the chatbot means that the changes you've made will be visible to users interacting with your chatbot. When you're ready to deploy your flow, click **Deploy** in the flow editor toolbar.

![](https://files.readme.io/b83e49e-deploy-flow.png)

__Updated 12 months ago

* * *

# Message Types

## 

Overview

BotBonnie supports different message types that you can add to your flow modules. Different message types are available for each channel type—refer to the table below for details.

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Tip
> 
> To learn more about each message types, click on its name.

Message Type| LINE| Messenger| Instagram| WebChat| WhatsApp| Kakao Sangdam Talk  
---|---|---|---|---|---|---  
[Text](doc:message-type-text)| ✓| ✓| ✓| ✓| ✓| ✓  
[Image](doc:message-type-image)| ✓| ✓| ✓| ✓| ✓| ✓  
[Carousel](doc:message-type-carousel)| ✓| ✓| ✓| ✓| Not supported| Not supported  
[Card](doc:message-type-card)| Not supported| Not supported| Not supported| Not supported| Not supported| ✓  
[Image carousel](doc:message-type-image-carousel)| ✓| Not supported| Not supported| Not supported| Not supported| Not supported  
[Video](doc:message-type-video)| ✓| ✓| ✓| ✓| ✓| Not supported  
[Audio](doc:message-type-audio)| ✓| ✓| Not supported| ✓| ✓| Not supported  
[Imagemap](doc:message-type-imagemap)| ✓| Not supported| Not supported| Not supported| Not supported| Not supported  
[Video imagemap](doc:message-type-video-imagemap)| ✓| Not supported| Not supported| Not supported| Not supported| Not supported  
[Delay](doc:message-type-delay)| Not supported| ✓| ✓| Not supported| Not supported| Not supported  
[One-time notification request](doc:message-type-one-time-notification-request)| ✓| ✓| Not supported| Not supported| Not supported| Not supported  
[Recurring notifications request](doc:message-type-recurring-notifications-request)| Not supported| ✓| Not supported| Not supported| Not supported| Not supported  
[JSON](doc:message-type-json)| ✓| ✓| ✓| Not supported| Not supported| Not supported  
  
 __Updated 9 months ago

# Getting Started with BotBonnie

## 

Overview

BotBonnie, by Appier, is an omnichannel chatbot platform designed to streamline your marketing efforts and enhance customer engagement through conversational marketing. BotBonnie integrates seamlessly with various platforms to deliver consistent and personalized interactions. Whether you aim to boost sales, improve customer service, or gather valuable insights, BotBonnie is here to simplify and optimize your processes. Use BotBonnie for:

  * Conversational marketing
  * Automated customer service
  * Comprehensive customer segmentation
  * Analytics and reporting

  
![](https://files.readme.io/f16494f-IG_adv_kits.png)

* * *

## 

Conversational marketing

### 

No-code, drag-and-drop flow builder

Utilize BotBonnie's intuitive drag-and-drop interface to design your chatbot. You can create messages using various formats such as text, images, videos, and carousels.

![](https://files.readme.io/a8c75e4-bb-getting-started-flow.png)

### 

Comprehensive collection of prebuilt marketing kits

Customize campaigns using an array of pre-built gamified marketing tools such as daily check-ins, lucky draws, scratch-off cards, and member-get-member programs to engage interactions.

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Tip
> 
> Automate activity rewards, dynamic discount codes, and coupons to incentivize engagement and drive conversions. Use BotBonnie's advanced kits to easily manage sweepstakes, follower acquisition, surveys, and invoice registration to boost participation and grow your audience.

![](https://files.readme.io/986bee9-luck-wheel-kit-settings.png)

### 

Omnichannel messaging center

BotBonnie supports integrations with your business's social media and messaging accounts including LINE, Facebook Messenger, Instagram, WhatsApp, KakaoTalk, Viber, and Zalo.

In addition, you can connect to your e-commerce platforms of choice, including Shopify, Shopline, 91 APP, and Cyberbiz, to send notifications for post-purchase journeys.

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Tip
> 
> Connect multiple LINE accounts and Facebook pages to a single bot to streamline operations and centralize communication. Filter customer support conversations by user nickname, tags, and message types, and respond to them on mobile just like chatting with friends for a more personal touch.

![](https://files.readme.io/e00d821-bb-getting-started-live-chat.png)

* * *

## 

Automated customer service

Configure proactive chat prompts to start conversations with visitors at the right moment, reducing bounce rates and encouraging conversions.

  * Growth tools allow you to acquire new customers from offline events, such as providing a QR code that users can scan during an in-person promotional event to add your brand's official account.
  * Broadcasts allow you to send important announcements, such as sales events, to your customers.
  * Automated responses and seamless handovers to human customer service agents ensure continuous and efficient support.



* * *

## 

Comprehensive customer segmentation

Collect and segment user data based on their interactions and behavior. Target these segments with personalized messages to maximize engagement and marketing returns.

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Tip
> 
> Collect user interaction data and profiles to enhance personalized services and improve customer satisfaction. BotBonnie allows you to efficiently manage user lists for ad placement and data integration to optimize marketing campaigns and drive better results.

* * *

## 

Analytics and reporting

Connect and unify data from various touchpoints (messaging apps, websites, e-commerce platforms) and use Appier’s AI models to analyze data and generate insightful reports.

![](https://files.readme.io/6d7d5b1-bb-getting-started-analytics.png)

* * *

Ready to start setting up your chatbot? Follow our [quick start guide](doc:quick-start-guide) for a step-by-step setup instructions.

__Updated 12 months ago

* * *

[Quick Start Guide](/docs/quick-start-guide)

# Getting Started with Growth Tools

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Supported channels
> 
>   * LINE
>   * Messenger
>   * Instagram
>   * Kakao
> 


Growth tools enable your brand to increase its friends and followers through interactions such as post replies, link clicks, or QR code scans. Growth tools are:

  * Conversation-starting entry points for users who can continue interacting with your brand and continue on to become friends or followers of your social channels.
  * Used to initiate conversations at various touchpoints, such as official websites, posts, offline event boards, and more, making it easier for users to join the channel.
  * Performance reports are available for growth tools to allowing you to understand their effectiveness.



* * *

## 

View and manage growth tools

After creating a growth tool, you'll be able to see the following basic information for each tool on the growth list page:

  * **Title**
  * **Type**
  * **Channel**
  * **Operational status**
  * **Operation period**



### 

Manage growth tools

Each growth tool will have icons next to it for copying its link, viewing its performance, editing, and deleting. To enable or disable a growth tool, toggle the **Operational status** switch.

![](https://files.readme.io/886fde8-growth-tool-operational-status.png)

In addition, you can utilize the search function at the top of the page to directly search for a growth tool by its name.

![](https://files.readme.io/845d8c7-growth-tool-search.png)

__Updated 2 months ago

* * *

# Getting Started with Journey Maps

Appier's journey maps use an omni-channel customer journey builder that allows you to quickly launch personalized, comprehensive campaigns across Appier's supported marketing channels. Journey maps are designed for:

  * **Cross-channel connection** : Connect user data across channels to create a holistic user profile and amplify your reach with a wide range of supported platforms including websites and mobile apps, conventional marketing channels such as email and SMS, and messaging channels like LINE and WebChat.
  * **User-based marketing** : Orchestrate user-based campaigns and track performance metrics for each touchpoint, allowing you to quickly identify steps in the journey that can be optimized to maximize conversions.
  * **Efficient journey map building** : With an intuitive interface, dozens of prebuilt templates, and our AI-powered Journey Copilot, you can build customer journeys with minimum effort.

![](https://files.readme.io/fe6e13b-journey-map.png)

### 

Main concepts

A journey map takes your users through a series of messages based on different conditions at each step of the journey. Users across different devices and channels are unified based on the unique identifier `user_id` to create a personalized and seamless experience.

Below are the main components of a journey map: **Trigger** , **Message** , **Split** , **Wait** , and **Exit**.

![](https://files.readme.io/db35eee-Screen_Shot_2023-11-14_at_6.39.35_PM.png)

  * A journey map starts with an entry **trigger** , which can be based on past conditions, real-time events, or a date-format user attribute. Users who meet the trigger criteria (e.g. signed up for membership) enter the journey.
  * Users are targeted with different **messages** based on the **split** path they are in (e.g. users who viewed tutorial videos vs users who didn't) and based on the **wait** time you set.
  * Users **exit** the journey when they reach an exit on the map or when they fulfill the exit criteria (e.g. users fulfilled the marketing goal by making a purchase).



* * *

## 

Requirements

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> Contact your customer success manager to activate this feature.

  * User data requirements
  * Channel integration



### 

User data requirements

Some features in journey maps have specific user data tracking requirements. Refer to the sections below.

  * Offline events and segments
  * Offline users
  * Date format data



#### 

Offline events and segments

In journey maps, you can use offline events when setting up trigger events and conditions. Offline events are identifiable by an **Offline** tag in journey analytics and can be selected as conversion goals alongside online events.

![](https://files.readme.io/29d6e0110df025867fd605e50eeca59910046bdae44ef739c996adff3c3d28f0-Offline_tag.png)

To use offline events or segments in journey maps:

  * The offline events must be uploaded through the [Offline Event API v2](https://docs.aiqua.appier.com/reference/getting-starting-with-the-offline-event-api-v2) with `user_id` as the unique identifier.
  * The offline segments must be created on AIQUA. The offline events must include `user_id` as the unique identifier. See [segment by offline events](https://docs.aiqua.appier.com/docs/segment-by-offline-events).



For details and best practices on using offline events and segments in journey maps, see [merging online and offline users](doc:merging-offline-and-online-user-data).

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Limitations
> 
>   * New offline users uploaded within one day may not be processed in time to be considered in the past conditions.
>   * Offline events cannot be used as real-time trigger events in in-web and in-app campaigns.
> 


#### 

Offline users

If you upload offline users using the [Bulk Upload Offline Users API](https://docs.aiqua.appier.com/reference/bulk-upload-offline-users) or [Add / Update User Profiles](https://docs.aiqua.appier.com/docs/updating-user-profiles) feature, be sure to include a `user_id` in each user record. Offline users need to have an `user_id` in order to enter a journey.

#### 

Tracking date format data

Your user attributes and events may include date-related data such as birthdays and departure dates. Journey maps will automatically convert event or attribute parameters that meet the following requirements to date-type data.

  * The value must follow this format: `YYYY-MM-DD`
  * The parameter name must satisfy one of the following requirements:
    * The parameter name is `birthday`.
    * The parameter name includes `_date`, for example, `departure_date` and `register_date_website`.



After the user data is converted to date format:

  * The following operators will be available when setting up conditions in journey maps: `in the past`, `in the next`, `before`, `after`, `on or before`, `on or after`, `during`, and `in the month of`.
  * Date-format user attributes can be used as a [date-based trigger](doc:entry-trigger-node#date-based) for users to enter the journey.



* * *

### 

Channel integration

Below are the channels you can use in journey maps. No additional integration is required if you are already using these channels on BotBonnie.

  * LINE
  * Facebook
  * WebChat
  * WhatsApp
  * Zalo
  * Viber



#### 

LINE integration

To use LINE campaigns in journey maps:

  * If you are already using LINE on BotBonnie, no additional integration is required.
  * If you have not set up LINE integration before or if you have previously set up LINE integration through AIQUA, you must integrate your LINE Official Account through the Appier Enterprise Console.
    * To integrate, log into Appier Enterprise Console (`https://console.appier.com/`) and follow the [LINE integration guide](doc:channels-line).
    * Existing LINE users and any associated user data from the AIQUA LINE integration will be synced in journey maps.



__Updated 6 months ago

* * *

# Go-To Module

## 

Overview

A message can be linked to a go-to module which is automatically sent after the initial message. Go-to modules can accomplish functions such as:

  * Automatically sending multiple modules consecutively to exceed the five message per module restriction (Note: Not applicable to LINE).
  * Automatically triggering rules kits (conditions, sequence) without requiring users to click a button or type a message.
  * Automatically switching between roles (persona kits), changing the chatbot's profile picture and name in the chatroom.



> 📘
> 
> A module can contain a go-to module or [quick reply](doc:quick-reply), but not both.

### 

LINE limitation

LINE only allows for five messages to be sent consecutively, regardless of whether the messages are contained in a single module or split between multiple modules.

For example, if you send four messages in one module, then add a go-to module that sends another four messages (for a total of eight messages), only the first five will be sent—the remaining three messages won't be sent.

  * First module: All four messages will be sent successfully
  * Go-to module: Only the first message will be sent, the last three won't be sent

![](https://files.readme.io/deadfc9-go-to-line-limitation.png)

* * *

## 

Adding a go-to module

  1. Open the module you'd like to link to a go-to module, then click **\+ Add Go-To Module**.

![](https://files.readme.io/4880517-add-go-to-module.png)

  2. In the dropdown menu, select the module you'd like to send immediately after the initial message.

![](https://files.readme.io/94ae698-set-go-to-module.png)

The go-to module that you configure will be sent immediately after the initial module is sent, without requiring any additional user interaction.

__Updated 12 months ago

# Greetings

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Supported channels
> 
>   * Facebook
> 


Greetings appears when users interact with a Facebook fan page bot via Messenger for the first time. Customize greetings to introduce your brand, describe available services, or guide users in interacting with the chatbot.

* * *

## 

Set a Greeting Message

  1. From the menu, go to **Content** > **Greetings**.

![](https://files.readme.io/30225acbc0153c907328c5776b3194933f53dcef4bed59ab2c206524403076e6-content-greetings.png)

  2. Enter a greeting message in the text input box. A preview will be displayed on the right.

![](https://files.readme.io/e6f7c372ec0dd5e793d285e1d7c96a5ecaa29fc14465d374d87ebad9d6eb477f-greetings-message.png)

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> Click **{...}** or the emoji icon at the bottom right to add dynamic content or emojis to create [personalize messages](doc:modules-and-messages#personalizing-messages).

  3. (Optional) Configure the **Get Started** button action:
     * Select a module from the dropdown to guide users with a welcome message, introduce your brand, direct users to your website, or enable chatbot interactions.
     * Select **None** if no further action is needed.

![](https://files.readme.io/67f57a01219c1d61ca3e63c5e40556dc5893ad5b2667bb9ee42fa7800e668b58-get-started-action.png)

  4. Click **Deploy bot** to save the greeting message.

![](https://files.readme.io/76d71aff7c07884fe9a44d3f3b9f0529d4ea5d07bcc0d22c291c4f856572e418-deploy-bot.png)

# LINE Smart Link

Use LINE smart links to execute a triggered action (e.g. updating parameters, tags, and demographic attributes) for a user when they click the URL. LINE smart links can be embedded, just like a standard URL, and can also be shared via QR code. After a user clicks a LINE smart link:

  1. The user will be briefly redirected to your LINE Official Account in the LINE app.
  2. Immediately after opening their conversation with your LINE Official Account, they will be automatically redirected to the destination URL you specify.



You can also place a LINE smart link outside the Official Account, e.g. a standard web page. When users who are friends of the LINE Official Account click on the link, their clicks will also be recorded.

### 

Prerequisites

Before creating a LINE smart link, you'll need to create a LIFF app on the LINE Developers Console under the same provider as your Official Account. Please note the following LIFF app requirements:

  * The LIFF app's **Size** setting must be set to **Compact**.
  * **Endpoint URL** must be set to: `https://rd.botbonnie.com/liff/<LINE_OA_ID>`, where `<LINE_OA_ID>` is the ID of the linked Official Account.
  * The following **Scopes** must be selected: **profile** , **openID**.



* * *

## 

Create a LINE smart link

  1. Go to **Growth tools** , click **\+ Add growth tool** , then select **LINE > LINE Smart Link**.

![](https://files.readme.io/867d4c4-nav-add-growth-tool.png)

  2. Enter a name for the LINE smart link campaign.
  3. Under **Operation period** , set the validity period of the link.
     1. **Permanent** : The link will immediately be active and will continue to function until it is deleted.
     2. **Limited-time** : The link is only valid during the specified time period.

![](https://files.readme.io/a87c8f4-line-smart-link-operation-period.png)

  4. Under **Redirect URL** , enter the final destination URL. Note that users who click on the LINE smart link will be briefly redirected to your LINE Official Account before being automatically redirected to the final destination URL.
  5. (Optional) Under **Triggered actions** , add the [triggered actions](doc:triggered-actions) you'd like to execute when the user clicks on the link. For example, you can update the user's parameters or tags.
  6. Under **Channel** , select the connected LINE Official Channel that should be opened when the smart link is clicked.

![](https://files.readme.io/2770157-line-smart-link-channel.png)

  7. Under **LIFF ID Setting - Compact** , the ID of your LIFF app will automatically be populated. If you encounter any issues, please contact your customer success manager for assistance.

![](https://files.readme.io/00b5242-line-smart-link-liff-id.png)

  8. After completing all the required settings, click **Generate** , then copy the short URL or download the QR code to use the LINE smart link.

![](https://files.readme.io/027bf69-line-smart-link-generate.png)

* * *

## 

Performance report

After publishing the smart link, you can open its performance report by clicking the eye icon in the growth tool list. The report includes:

  * The total number of unique users who clicked a URL or scanned a QR code.
  * The total number of URL clicks and QR code scans.
  * Line charts showing the trends of clicks and scans.



> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> The report includes clicks and QR scans from both new and existing friends.

![](https://files.readme.io/3dabf23-smart-link-report-performance.png)

* * *

## 

LINE smart link FAQs

### 

If I have already created the LINE smart link and made it public, can I still edit it?

Yes, you can update the URL without changing the channel, and it won't affect URLs and QR codes that have already been published.

### 

Why are some clicks not recorded?

When users click on a link, they can still be redirected to the redirect page even if they don’t become friends of the official account or grant permission for the LINE LIFF app to access their personal information. However, BotBonnie can't tag them or record the click count under these circumstances.

![](https://files.readme.io/3d4597f-image5.png)

# Start Conversation with Short Link

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Supported channels
> 
>   * LINE
>   * Messenger
>   * Instagram
>   * Kakao
> 


Use BotBonnie's short link and QR code generator to increase your brand account's friends and followers by providing an easy entry point for users to engage with your brand.

* * *

## 

Start conversation with a short link or QR code

  1. Go to **Growth tools** , and then click **\+ Add growth tool**.

![](https://files.readme.io/e8d035288e78a19f9a5d57f498aded8a4d4335b8c5a9a8c6c74ce0048a090a94-growth-tool-add.png)

  2. Choose the channel for this growth tool, and then select **Start conversation with short link**.

![](https://files.readme.io/dd38d46cfc19af954cd605d59a73a0593c390ca4e3d9baf9e636d0c340255158-growth-tool-select-channel.png)

  3. In the setup page, complete the following settings:
     * **Campaign name** : Enter an identifiable and manageable campaign name. This name won't be visible to users.
     * **Operation period** : Choose **Permanent** or select **Limited-time** and specify a **Start time** and **End time** for the growth tool.
     * **Started conversation module** : Select the [module](doc:modules-and-messages) that opens when the link is clicked. For a module to be available for selection, it needs to be created and published in the flow beforehand.
     * **Triggered actions** : Select a [triggered action](doc:triggered-actions) to execute when the user clicks the link.
     * **Channel** : Select the specific account for this tool. Only connected messaging accounts are displayed.



> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> Don't modify the **LIFF ID Setting - Compact**.

![](https://files.readme.io/da6dd58-url-qr-code-settings.png)

  4. After you complete all the settings, click **Generate**.
  5. After generation, the URL and QR code appear in the **URL setting** and **QR code**. Click **Save** to apply your settings. You can now:
     * Share the URL by clicking the copy icon.
     * Click to download the QR code to share with users.

![](https://files.readme.io/cbc4503-url-qr-code-generate-save.png)

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Edit the growth tool
> 
> You can edit the growth tool and regenerate a URL and QR code. The previous URL and QR code continue to function as long as the channel hasn't been changed.
> 
> Note: For Kakao growth tools, the URL in the **URL setting** field is fixed and can't be customized.

* * *

## 

View performance report

After you've made the URL or QR code publicly accessible, you can monitor their effectiveness by viewing the performance page.

Go to the growth tool list, and click the eye icon on the right side to check the report. The performance report shows:

  * Total unique users who clicked the URL or scanned the QR code
  * Total number of clicks or scans
  * Line charts that display engagement trends over time

![](https://files.readme.io/7596e70-url-qr-code-report.png)

To view data for a specific time period, use the date selector to filter the report. This helps you analyze engagement patterns of your growth tool across different periods effectively.

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> The report includes data from both new users and existing friends who click the URL or scan the QR code.

__Updated about 2 months ago

* * *

# Journey Copilot

## 

Overview

After [adding a new journey map from scratch](doc:choose-a-journey-creation-method), you can use journey copilot, your AI-powered assistant, to automatically generate a journey map based on a text description of the desired user journey, for example:

  * "Drive newsletter sign-ups for exclusive product launch notifications."
  * "Increase user engagement by promoting participation in a seasonal winter sales event with limited-time discounts."
  * "Boost customer retention by encouraging recent purchasers to join the loyalty program, offering points for every purchase, and promoting upcoming exclusive member events and sales."



After copilot generates a journey, you can review the nodes and settings before applying the changes to your journey map.

![](https://files.readme.io/1140752-jm-copilot-prompt.png)

* * *

## 

Using journey copilot

### 

1\. Open journey copilot

Open the journey map you'd like to edit and click the copilot icon at the top right corner of the canvas.

![](https://files.readme.io/1156b09-jm-open-copilot.png)

### 

2\. Enter a journey description

In the copilot window, enter a description of the desired user journey.

![](https://files.readme.io/a0b75dc-jm-copilot-prompt.png)

If you're not sure how to write a description, click **Try example** for a list of suggestions. You can click on any example to generate a journey based on that description.

![](https://files.readme.io/ff122e5-jm-copilot-example.png)

### 

3\. Generate and apply copilot's suggestions

Next, click **Generate** to see what the copilot-generated journey looks like.

![](https://files.readme.io/fc129c2-jm-generate-copilot-journey.png)

If the resulting journey settings are suitable for your marketing scenario, click **Apply** to add the journey to the canvas.

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> The journey generated by journey copilot won't be applied to the canvas until you click **Apply**.

![](https://files.readme.io/163f8f3-jm-apply-copilot-journey.png)

The copilot-generated journey is now saved, and you can continue to [add and edit journey nodes](doc:add-journey-nodes) or directly proceed to [configure the journey's schedule and re-entry settings](doc:journey-schedule-and-re-entry-settings).

__Updated 7 months ago

* * *

[Choose a Journey Creation Method](/docs/choose-a-journey-creation-method)[Add Journey Nodes](/docs/add-journey-nodes)

# Journey Map FAQs

## 

Performance and Analytics

  * What counts as one user in a journey?
  * Are re-entered users counted again in node stats?
  * Why is "Opens" under Performance much higher than "Opened" in node stats?
  * Is "Arrived" the number of users who received the message?



### 

What counts as one user in a journey?

In journey maps, users across different channels are unified based on the unique identifier `user_id`, which is the custom user ID used by your company.

For example, if a person logs in to your website, this web user is no longer an anonymous visitor and can be linked to a `user_id`. If this person has installed your Android app and logged in on the app, this web user can then be unified with the Android user based on the same `user_id`.

If two different devices with the same `user_id` arrive at a node, they will be deduplicated and count as one user in the node statistics.

* * *

### 

Are re-entered users counted again in node stats?

Yes. The user counts are counted based on the number of entries. For example, if a user exited the journey and then re-entered one time, this user is counted 2 times under Entered.

* * *

### 

Why is "Opens" under Performance much higher than "Opened" in node stats?

You may find the message metrics count under the **Performance** tab to be much higher than the metrics count under **Moved forward to**.

This is because users might still interact (e.g. opens, clicks) with the messages after being moved to the no-interaction path. This can often happen If you set a short timeout duration.

![](https://files.readme.io/e43437d-Screen_Shot_2023-10-31_at_7.04.00_PM.png)

In the example below, users are moved to the "Not opened" path if they didn't open the email in one hour. But if they open the email while they are still in the journey, the open will be counted in the Performance tab.

![](https://files.readme.io/044e61d-Screen_Shot_2023-10-31_at_7.09.33_PM.png)

* * *

### 

Is "Arrived" the number of users who received the message?

In message nodes, **Arrived** is the number of users the system has tried to send or show the message to. This is not the number of users who received the message.

To see the actual sent, views, delivered, and clicks metrics, open the message node and see the **Performance** tab instead.

__Updated 12 months ago

* * *

[Journey Performance and Analytics](/docs/journey-performance-and-analytics)[Use Cases](/docs/journey-map-use-cases)

# Merging Offline and Online User Data

## 

Overview

Journey maps allow you to connect online and offline user data to create a seamless experience for your customers. You can upload offline events that occurred in your physical stores, such as purchases and membership registrations, and use these events in journey maps.

See Use case: encourage offline purchase for an example.

### 

Requirements

In journey maps, users across different channels are unified based on `user_id`. To learn more about the requirements and limitations of using offline events in journey maps, see the requirements for [offline events and segments](doc:getting-started-with-journey-maps#offline-events-and-segments).

* * *

## 

Best practices

Follow the best practices below to ensure offline events are effectively uploaded and used in journey maps.

  * Upload events frequently
  * Add an upload time interval



### 

Upload events frequently

Unlike online events tracked via Appier SDK, offline events need to be uploaded to AIQUA via APIs. As a result, there will always be a time gap between when the event actually occurred and when the event is uploaded.

We recommend uploading offline events at least once a day if you want to use offline events or segments in journey maps.

### 

Add an upload time interval to journey flow

In some nodes, you can extend the time periods to allow more time for offline events to be uploaded.

  * **Past condition** : Add the upload time interval to the time period you want to set in **Only consider events in the last x days**. For example, if your event upload interval is one day and you want to consider events in the last 3 days, set the **Only consider events in the last x days** settings to four days.

![](https://files.readme.io/88640d64f632aa7d5cdf9b563321dc68e682cc456b783f31a697b6067e9dc1fb-consider-events.png)

  * **Real-time event** : The event timestamp must be later than the journey activation time, and the users will enter the journey after the offline events are uploaded.
  * **Wait for conditions:** Extend the timeout period by adding the event upload interval. For example, if your event upload interval is one day, and you want to wait seven days for users to complete the conditions, set the timeout period to eight days.

![](https://files.readme.io/1351500-Screen_Shot_2023-11-27_at_6.05.08_PM.png)

  * **Check condition** : Insert a time delay node before the check condition node to make sure offline events are already uploaded before users proceed to the check condition node. For example, if you upload offline events once a day, you can add a time delay node to put users on hold for a day.

![](https://files.readme.io/1f6d30b-check_condition_time_delay.png)

  * **Goal event** : Goal events are calculated based on the actual timestamp of the offline events. The event count will be added after the event upload, but the end results are not affected.
  * **Exit rule** : Users who meet the exit rule will exit the journey after the exit events are uploaded.



* * *

## 

Use Case: Encourage offline purchases

Let's say you want to send marketing campaigns to the VIP members to encourage offline purchases in your physical stores.

First, make sure offline purchase events are uploaded to AIQUA daily.

Next, set up the journey map as below.

  1. In the entry trigger node, select **Past condition** and set the condition to include VIP members.
  2. Add a **Wait for condition** node and add the offline purchase event as the condition.
  3. In the **Matched** path, add a thank-you email to users who have completed offline purchases.
  4. In the **Timeout** path, email encourage offline purchases.
  5. If the email is opened, send another email to follow up. If the email is not opened, send a message through SMS instead.
  6. Set the offline purchase event as the goal event and exit rule.

![](https://files.readme.io/b30cd8d4fb3d8e254b8934ec670e1dc611421b12eaca7d7a027609c186cdd888-offline-purchase.png)

__Updated 12 months ago

* * *

# Advanced Node

## 

Overview

Use advanced nodes to implement more complex functionalities in your journey, such as integrating with external APIs and services.

  * Webhook node

![](https://files.readme.io/f35e50d91c0612a3fe007d49d62124dc82c149c7527f5f34a536e17b38c22a30-advanced-node-menu.png)

* * *

## 

Webhook node

A webhook is an automated HTTP request sent by from one service to another when a specific event occurs. You can use webhooks to enhance your journey by integrating external APIs and services, enabling you to personalize the next steps in the journey.

When a user reaches the webhook node, an HTTP request is triggered to an external service to retrieve data which can be used to tailor the campaign in real time, supporting use cases such as:

  * Fetching location-based data such as weather.
  * Retrieving customer profile details.
  * Checking inventory status.



To create a webhook node, click on any **+** icon that appears between and after nodes, then click **Advanced > Webhook**.

![](https://files.readme.io/4debc10344b71029a4b8c3ba9f3d40ef6ce9b351de6fcb22a08c0acd9f0646b3-add-webhook-node.png)

Clicking the newly added node opens the settings panel with the following fields:

Name| Required| Description| Example  
---|---|---|---  
**Node name**|  Yes| Enter a name for this node.| "Send coupon"  
**Target URL**|  Yes| Enter the API's endpoint URL.| `https://www.example.com/coupon_endpoint`  
**HTTP method**|  Yes| Select an HTTP method from the dropdown.| `GET`  
**QPS**|  No| Enter the maximum number of queries to send.| `10`  
**Batch requests enabled**  
  
**Batch size**  
  
**Batch – Minimum interval**|  No| Batch multiple API calls into a single request.   
• **Batch requests enabled** : To enable batching, enter "true" into the input field.   
  
• **Batch size** : Enter the maximum number of requests to batch together.   
  
• **Batch – Minimum interval** : Specify the interval (in milliseconds) at which the batch request will be sent, regardless if the batch size has been met.| ![](https://files.readme.io/c0b81dcd42fc029f69feb6c39c11a56bf58a64a66fd1c43934aa2b7c2942cab4-webhook-settings-batch-request.png)  
**HTTP header**|  No| Enter any required HTTP headers.| `{"Authorization": "Bearer TOKEN"}`  
**Content type**|  No| Specify the request's content type.| `application/json`  
**Request body**|  No| Enter the request body content.   
  
To dynamically insert user attributes in the request body, use `{{ }}`. For example, to insert the value of the user's `email` attribute, use `{{email}}`.| `{"membership_email":"{{email}}"}`  
**Response field mapping**|  No| Create a mapping between webhook response field names and the name you'd like to use when creating conditions or for use in dynamic content.   
Tip: Use dot notation to map nested fields.| `{"coupon.code": "coupon_code", "coupon.discount_pct": "discount"}`  
  
After completing the webhook settings, you can:

  * Create conditions based on webhook responses which determine the path users should take next in the journey.
  * Use webhook fields to populate dynamic content in the journey's subsequent campaigns.



### 

Creating conditions based on webhook responses

Select **Check response** to start creating scenarios to split users by. In a scenario, you can add one or more conditions based on the webhook's responses.

![](https://files.readme.io/fcdf5f1975d8968359699b8f13851f511d3fed926ee8fad15c2c98a6e9c3360b-webhook-check-response.png)

In each scenario, next to **Match** , determine whether users need to satisfy all conditions or any conditions in the scenario to split into the scenario.

![](https://files.readme.io/d4914b6362fa42f70c3188f9fc186437ace541cd8c18e466c5226c9f4d9a8f2d-webhook-scenario.png)

Next, for each condition in the scenario, configure the following:

  1. Field name: Enter the name of the field in the webhook response.
  2. Data type: Select the field's data type.
  3. Operator: Select the operator used to evaluate the field's value. The data type you selected determines what operators are available.
  4. Value: Enter the value to compare the field's value against.



After adding the desired scenarios and conditions, click **Save**.

![](https://files.readme.io/bd043bbf0d12c3880c5820c4e69a665fabfb88d6335bd2aaf792254549f991a3-webhook-conditions.png)

### 

Using webhook fields to populate dynamic content

Use the syntax provided in the following table to insert dynamic content in your campaign. Note that the syntax will differ depending on the campaign channel.

Campaign channel| Dynamic content syntax  
---|---  
• Web push   
• App push   
• Email   
• SMS   
• In-app   
• In-web| `{{ctx_<WEBHOOK_RESPONSE_FIELD_NAME>}}`   
Replace `<WEBHOOK_RESPONSE_FIELD_NAME>` with one of the values you specified in the response field mapping.  
![](https://files.readme.io/f63dfe84f84448d3f0c670764526ef5898deb7c4d769dde869dcbd430b3cb388-aq-dynamic-content.png)  
• LINE  
• WebChat  
• WhatsApp  
• Zalo  
• Viber| `{{extra_params_<WEBHOOK_RESPONSE_FIELD_NAME>}}`  
  
Replace `<WEBHOOK_RESPONSE_FIELD_NAME>` with one of the values you specified in the response field mapping.  
![](https://files.readme.io/43cd50d24c8f213400ba412fac26d22e7e59acd26d1f032390365dcbb900e02e-bb-dynamic-content.png)![](https://files.readme.io/43cd50d24c8f213400ba412fac26d22e7e59acd26d1f032390365dcbb900e02e-bb-dynamic-content)  
  
__Updated 5 months ago

# Journey Performance and Analytics

After a journey has started running, you can see the performance and analytics of the journey map. Refer to the sections below.

**How users flow through the journey**

  * Journey overview
  * Node details (Node stats)



**Campaign performance and experiment results**

  * Node details (Performance)



**Overall journey performance**

  * Journey analytics
  * Journey map list



> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> Metrics related to **Offline conversions** appear only if your journey includes offline events. By default, these columns remain hidden until you publish a journey with offline events enabled.

* * *

## 

Journey overview

You can click the journey to see how the users move through the journey under the **Journey overview** tab.

![](https://files.readme.io/5016d29-Screen_Shot_2023-10-19_at_2.51.39_PM.png)

Under the nodes, the following metrics are available:

  * **Entered** : The number of users who met the trigger condition and started the journey by entering the entry node. Re-entered users are counted each time they enter.
  * **Arrived** : The number of users who arrived at this node. In message nodes, this is the number of users the system has tried to send the message to. To see the actual sent, views, delivered, and clicked metrics, open the message node and see the **Performance** tab instead.
  * **Moved forward to** : The number of users who have moved on to the next node. If there are multiple paths after this node, you can click the expand arrow to see the user counts for each path. In message nodes, if the user interaction (e.g. a click) happens after the timeout period has passed, the user will still remain in the no interaction path (e.g. not clicked path).
  * **On hold** : The number of users who are on hold at this node for a fixed duration or until a time slot.
  * **Wait for response** : The number of users who are kept at the message node until they interact with the message or until timeout.
  * **Exited journey** : The number of users who exited the journey at this node. Click the expand arrow to see the following numbers:
    * **Exit rule met** : The number of users who exited the journey by completing the exit event specified in the exit rule.
    * **Dropped** : The number of users who exited the journey because they were not reachable in the channel. For example, users who entered the email message path in the journey but do not have a valid email address.
  * **Complete** : The number of users who completed and exited the journey because they reached an exit node in the flow.



### 

Node details

Click on a node to open a panel containing several tabs with different types of node details.

Tab| Description  
---|---  
**Node overview**|  Shows the summary of the node conditions or message content.  
**Node stats**|  Shows the number of users who arrived at the node, moved forward to the next node, are currently being held at the node, and exited the journey while they are at the node.  
**Performance** (Experiment node)| Shows the traffic allocation and user count and conversion metrics for each path. For nodes with the control group enabled, conversion rate uplift (**CVR uplift**) is also shown.![](https://files.readme.io/b40121c20a0811465811be33e88200426ad3a892257ca6138ae0cb6bfe4d082b-experiment-performance-node.png)You can change the attribution model of an active journey from the **Settings** page, but note that doing so will clear existing node performance data for the journey.![](https://files.readme.io/987c83ea62b78d7c470b084aab3435b11dd234fe9f5ab10e470e517356845883-ab-testing-node-attribution-model-setting.png)  
**Performance** (Message node)| Shows delivery status, campaign performance data, and a detailed breakdown of conversion metrics.![](https://files.readme.io/26ebdcfcfb6cc1f719523a38d59d24b38811f8d9cff6b03d0c154a6bebdd6887-nodes-performance.png)  
  
* * *

## 

Journey analytics

Open the journey and click the **Journey analytics** tab to view key performance metrics and visualized trends for conversions, conversions value, total exits, and total clicks of your journey.

Using the date range selector and interval drop down menu to analyze data across different time periods.

![](https://files.readme.io/79c258be3e45bc918dccc72bbe4d4351de9e95ea0151bca35bc6672eebd30ca4-journey-map-journey-analytics.png)

* * *

## 

Journey map list

In the journey map list, you can see the following performance metrics of the journeys.

  * **Entered** : The number of users who met the trigger condition and started the journey. Re-entered users are counted each time they enter.
  * **Conversion** : The total number of conversion events completed by users who entered the journey.
  * **Conversion rate** : Calculated using (Conversions / Entered) x 100%.
  * **Conversion value** : The total value of online conversions from all channels in the journey.
  * **Offline conversions** : The total number of offline conversion events completed by users who entered the journey.
  * **Offline conversion rate** : Calculated using (Offline conversion events / Entered) x 100%.
  * **Offline conversion value** : The total value of offline conversions from all channels in the journey.
  * **Exit rule met** : The number of users who exited the journey by completing the exit event specified in the exit rule.
  * **Completed** : The number of users who finished the journey after reaching the last node of a path, excluding the not-reachable paths.
  * **Dropped** : The number of users who exited the journey because they were not reachable in the channel. For example, users who entered the email message path in the journey but do not have a valid email address.

![](https://files.readme.io/5ed41675af1cbc58b3877f5ec033295d48ac28f1f303fc6ba757ae430de7266b-JM_list.png)

* * *

## 

FAQ

See FAQs related to analytics and performance [here](doc:journey-map-faqs).

# Journey Schedule and Re-entry Settings

After you finish designing the journey map by [adding journey nodes](doc:add-journey-nodes), click **Next** to complete the journey settings.

Under **Journey schedule** , set the start time and the end time of the journey.

![](https://files.readme.io/61eeecb-Screen_Shot_2023-10-19_at_11.53.36_AM.png)

Under **Journey re-entry** , select a re-entry rule.

  * **Users can enter this journey only once** : Once exited, users will not re-enter this journey.
  * **Users can re-enter this journey up to X times after exiting** : Enter the maximum number of times users can enter this journey and set a minimum interval between entries.
  * **Users can re-enter this journey any number of times after exiting** : Users can re-enter this journey an unlimited number of times during the journey schedule. Set a minimum interval between entries.

![](https://files.readme.io/a9046f6-Screen_Shot_2023-10-19_at_2.08.48_PM.png)

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
>   * If you allow users to re-enter the journey, be sure to set an appropriate minimum interval to avoid sending too many messages to users. After the minimum interval has passed, users will re-enter the journey if the past conditions are met or when they complete the real-time event.
>   * If you edit the re-entry rule after a journey becomes active, users who are already in the journey but have exceeded the maximum number of re-entries will continue to finish the journey.
>   * Push notifications in journey map are also capped by the [daily limit](https://docs.aiqua.appier.com/docs/user-settings#daily-limit) set in account settings. The [minimum intervals](https://docs.aiqua.appier.com/docs/user-settings#minimum-interval) in account settings do not affect journey map messages.
> 


__Updated 12 months ago

# Kit Report

## 

Overview

BotBonnie automatically creates performance reports for the advanced kits used in your chatbot flow. These reports provide insights into key metrics like participant numbers, lucky draw statuses, and new follower gains.

![](https://files.readme.io/95b138d-kit-report.png)

* * *

## 

Viewing kit reports

Go to **Analytics > Kit Report** to see a list of all the kit reports for your advanced kits.

  * To open a report, click the eye icon on the right.
  * To open the flow analysis, click the three dots, then select **View Flow Insights**.
  * To open the kit in your flow, click the three dots, then select **View the flows**.

![](https://files.readme.io/4d169f4-kit-report-list.png) ![](https://files.readme.io/a840405-kit-report-list-2.png)

__Updated 12 months ago

* * *
