# Merging Offline and Online User Data

## 

Overview

Journey maps allow you to connect online and offline user data to create a seamless experience for your customers. You can upload offline events that occurred in your physical stores, such as purchases and membership registrations, and use these events in journey maps.

See Use case: encourage offline purchase for an example.

### 

Requirements

In journey maps, users across different channels are unified based on `user_id`. To learn more about the requirements and limitations of using offline events in journey maps, see the requirements for [offline events and segments](doc:getting-started-with-journey-maps#offline-events-and-segments).

* * *

## 

Best practices

Follow the best practices below to ensure offline events are effectively uploaded and used in journey maps.

  * Upload events frequently
  * Add an upload time interval



### 

Upload events frequently

Unlike online events tracked via Appier SDK, offline events need to be uploaded to AIQUA via APIs. As a result, there will always be a time gap between when the event actually occurred and when the event is uploaded.

We recommend uploading offline events at least once a day if you want to use offline events or segments in journey maps.

### 

Add an upload time interval to journey flow

In some nodes, you can extend the time periods to allow more time for offline events to be uploaded.

  * **Past condition** : Add the upload time interval to the time period you want to set in **Only consider events in the last x days**. For example, if your event upload interval is one day and you want to consider events in the last 3 days, set the **Only consider events in the last x days** settings to four days.

![](https://files.readme.io/88640d64f632aa7d5cdf9b563321dc68e682cc456b783f31a697b6067e9dc1fb-consider-events.png)

  * **Real-time event** : The event timestamp must be later than the journey activation time, and the users will enter the journey after the offline events are uploaded.
  * **Wait for conditions:** Extend the timeout period by adding the event upload interval. For example, if your event upload interval is one day, and you want to wait seven days for users to complete the conditions, set the timeout period to eight days.

![](https://files.readme.io/1351500-Screen_Shot_2023-11-27_at_6.05.08_PM.png)

  * **Check condition** : Insert a time delay node before the check condition node to make sure offline events are already uploaded before users proceed to the check condition node. For example, if you upload offline events once a day, you can add a time delay node to put users on hold for a day.

![](https://files.readme.io/1f6d30b-check_condition_time_delay.png)

  * **Goal event** : Goal events are calculated based on the actual timestamp of the offline events. The event count will be added after the event upload, but the end results are not affected.
  * **Exit rule** : Users who meet the exit rule will exit the journey after the exit events are uploaded.



* * *

## 

Use Case: Encourage offline purchases

Let's say you want to send marketing campaigns to the VIP members to encourage offline purchases in your physical stores.

First, make sure offline purchase events are uploaded to AIQUA daily.

Next, set up the journey map as below.

  1. In the entry trigger node, select **Past condition** and set the condition to include VIP members.
  2. Add a **Wait for condition** node and add the offline purchase event as the condition.
  3. In the **Matched** path, add a thank-you email to users who have completed offline purchases.
  4. In the **Timeout** path, email encourage offline purchases.
  5. If the email is opened, send another email to follow up. If the email is not opened, send a message through SMS instead.
  6. Set the offline purchase event as the goal event and exit rule.

![](https://files.readme.io/b30cd8d4fb3d8e254b8934ec670e1dc611421b12eaca7d7a027609c186cdd888-offline-purchase.png)

__Updated 12 months ago

# Message Node

## 

Overview

Message nodes allow you to reach out to users across all your integrated channels. Depending on the channel you select, you can either add a creative or a chatbot flow module.

  * Adding a message node
  * Set the split criteria



* * *

## 

Adding a message node

Click on any **+** icon that appears between and after nodes, then click **Channels** , and select the channel you'd like to use to send the message.

![](https://files.readme.io/c97e268-message-node-channel.png)

Next, enter a node name and set up the creatives or flow modules, depending on whether you selected a message node for an AIQUA channel or BotBonnie channel.

### 

Message node: AIQUA channel

If you add a message node for an AIQUA channel, you'll see a button to add a creative.

> 👍
> 
> To learn more about each creative and its settings, refer to the table of [creative types](https://docs.aiqua.appier.com/docs/creatives#creative-types).

![](https://files.readme.io/58f6f75-message-node-aq-channel.png)

For app push channels (Android and iOS), you also have the option to use advanced templates. To view the available advanced templates, select **Use advanced Android template** or **Use advanced iOS template**.

![](https://files.readme.io/a439f5a-push-advanced-templates.png)

### 

Message node: BotBonnie channel

If you add a message node for a BotBonnie channel, you'll see a button to add a flow module.

> 👍
> 
> To learn more about creating modules, see [Modules and Messages](https://docs.botbonnie.appier.com/docs/modules-and-messages).

![](https://files.readme.io/14f5c9b-message-node-bb-channel.png)

* * *

### 

Using dynamic content in message node

Dynamic content in message nodes enables you to create personalized interactions by customizing messages based on available user data. To insert personalized content, click the **{⋯}** icon in supported creative fields.

![](https://files.readme.io/603b88f3ad9d4694132e3b170a208d20e4dd636c80fb4eb2981c7cac9b87b831-bb-dynamic-content.png)

#### 

Support channels and data sources

BotBonnie channels support user attributes and events for dynamic content personalization. Note that only data collected through BotBonnie can be used.

#### 

Supported channels

  * LINE
  * Facebook Messenger
  * WhatsApp
  * WebChat
  * Viber
  * Zalo



> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> Kakao Talk currently does not support dynamic content.

#### 

Available data types

  * **User demographics** collected through BotBonnie.
  * **Custom parameters** created in BotBonnie.
  * **User events** , including:
    * Cart items (from `product_added_to_cart` events)
    * Browsed items (from `product_viewed` events)
    * Purchased items (from `product_purchased` events)

![](https://files.readme.io/7e2d0cf2b3a0b6ee614494d37b3e476c112c05c3b4a0c399ab6e8d852dac11f3-whitelisted-user-event.png)

* * *

## 

Set the split criteria

Users who receive the message will be split into two paths depending on whether the message was sent, or clicked. You can click the dropdown menu to change the split criteria.

![](https://files.readme.io/a862b5f83f238aa113b502df9a141417fa2f585183961690a0299d28beae5761-bb-split-node.png)

__Updated 12 months ago

* * *

[Entry Trigger Node](/docs/entry-trigger-node)[Split Node](/docs/split-node)

# Audio

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Supported channels
> 
>   * Messenger
>   * LINE
>   * WebChat
>   * WhatsApp
> 


You can send audio messages by uploading an audio file.

![](https://files.readme.io/ad394cd9c062677b6c0e9622e566582fe353ce894cca9a01483f9e58d8a8da61-audio-message.png)

* * *

## 

Create an audio message

Follow these steps to create an audio message:

  1. In **Flow** , click **Add module** , then select **Audio**.

![](https://files.readme.io/b2ddf0dcfaf31fea36dbb8f1aa1af33d26b63a76b05a8a3df2e4940df471e0f9-audio.png)

  2. Click the **Upload audio here** area. Ensure your audio file meets the following specifications:
     * Maximum file size: 15 MB
     * Supported formats: MP3, M4A

![](https://files.readme.io/a608ca52988e8a0e37ee28896f490eedcc218e4c4caad1967517ff9f01eb45a5-upload-audio.png)

__Updated 7 months ago

* * *

# Card

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Supported channels
> 
>   * Kakao Sangdam Talk
> 


Card messages contain an image, a button, a title, and an optional description.

![](https://files.readme.io/fe83f57-kakao-card.png)

## 

Requirements and limitations

  * The title is required, and can contain up to 300 characters.
  * The description can contain up to 600 characters.
  * A maximum of 5 buttons can be added.
  * Card image files must meet the following requirements:
    * File format must be PNG or JPG.
    * File size must not exceed 500 KB.
    * Width must be greater than 500 px.
    * Aspect ratio should be no greater than 2:1 and no less than 3:4.

# Carousel

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Supported channels
> 
>   * Messenger
>   * Instagram
>   * LINE
>   * WebChat
> 


Carousel messages display a series of cards in a horizontally-scrollable format. Each card can contain an image with an embedded link, title, and subtitle, as well as up to three buttons.

![](https://files.readme.io/72ce74afae038c4ca9382712e7e11985973ef0f89584b40c42704b1a882a709f-carousel-line-sample.png)

* * *

## 

Requirements and limitations

  * A carousel can contain up to 10 cards.
  * Carousel cards must meet the following requirements:
    * Supported format: PNG or JPEG
    * Maximum file size: 1 MB
    * Aspect ratio: 1.91:1 for standard view or 1:1 for square view
  * All cards in a single carousel must use the same view (standard or square).
  * A carousel card can contain up to three buttons.
  * Each carousel card must have a title and also contain at least one of the following: An image, a subtitle, or a button.



* * *

## 

Add a carousel card

To add a carousel card, click the **+** icon on the right side of the last card of the carousel.

![](https://files.readme.io/ed2a843d1f8018e1d9727e57f8b556d4d06aec12a7ca5b53be74feb85b2cbf4a-carousel-add-card.png)

After adding multiple cards, you'll be able to navigate to the edit screens of each carousel card by clicking the arrows on the left and right of the settings window.

![](https://files.readme.io/af0ef45d03e3153e35c04c7e7246a012183a503f5f0e521db09e1108262d546c-carousel-view-card.png)

* * *

## 

Resize carousel card images

Hover over a card's image and click the resize icon to toggle between standard view (1.91:1) and square view (1:1). All cards in the carousel update automatically to match.

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> Upload images with the correct aspect ratio to prevent unwanted cropping.

![](https://files.readme.io/9520825d51a19304de8ce6d15a7a847c88cdd5cb566545c59be5fd2074c4afa1-carousel-resize.png)

* * *

## 

Change card sequence

To change the sequence of a carousel card, click and drag the colored box below the card. The gray boxes represent the other cards in the carousel—use these as a reference to reposition the current card (the colored box) into the desired position in the sequence.

![](https://files.readme.io/32df9b96546b914d72661ec12f36ff683338ebd4938ab4f894df483dec17537b-carousel-click-to-reorder.png)

* * *

## 

Delete a card or a carousel

To delete either a single carousel card or to delete the entire carousel message, click the delete button and select one of the following options:

  * **Delete this card** : Delete the current carousel card.
  * **Delete this carousel** : Delete the entire carousel message.



> 🚧
> 
> ### 
> 
> Caution
> 
>   * If you delete a card, all of its settings will be irrecoverable.
>   * If you delete a carousel, the settings for all of the cards in the carousel will be irrecoverable.
> 


![](https://files.readme.io/fbfbb5f86a4487239fdce06a502c6eb9ea4b506c7cbc36fe10c7a5bd0e1f0aaa-carousel-delete-card-carousel.png)

# Delay

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Supported channels
> 
>   * Messenger
>   * Instagram
> 


Delay messages help control the timing of messages, making conversations feel more natural and engaging. You can use delays to simulate real typing speed, give users time to process previous messages, or pace interactions effectively.

* * *

## 

Create a delay message

Follow these steps to create a delay message:

  1. Go to **Flow** > **Add module** , then select **Delay**.

![](https://files.readme.io/b5053da8d0b9bb4ae88540d7af5e3bed75880acc8448640507e32d3aa7174d08-delay-message.png)

  2. Use the slider to set a delay between 1 and 20 seconds, in increments of 0.5 seconds.

![](https://files.readme.io/f740283b1f64285c1b30c38d67a8fc3f9995491783c2ddc1027d742353cfcd10-screenrecording0312.gif)

* * *

## 

Usage tips

  * Use delay messages to make conversations feel more natural, like simulating typing or giving users a moment to enjoy images or carousels before moving on to the next message.
  * Place delay message between different message in a module to improve flow.
  * Adjust the delay time based on the context. For text-based responses, a short delay is enough. For more complex messages, such as carousels or videos, a slightly longer delay can help users engage with the content.

# Image

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Supported channels
> 
> Image messages are available for all messaging channels.

Image messages contain a single image.

![](https://files.readme.io/fc962f3-image-message.png)

* * *

## 

Requirements and limitations

  * Supported formats: JPG, GIF, or PNG
  * Maximum file size: 1 MB



* * *

## 

Usage tips

To avoid issues with image display (for example, cropping), use an image aspect ratio that suits your message's placement and format. Common aspect ratios include: 1.91:1, 16:9, 1:1, 4:5, and 9:16.

# Image Carousel

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Supported channels
> 
>   * LINE
> 


Image carousel messages display a series of cards in a horizontally scrollable format, where each card can contain an image with a button or customizable interactive action regions.

Note that image carousel messages differ from standard [carousel](doc:message-type-carousel) messages. To learn more about which message type is more suitable for your use case, see the image carousel vs carousel comparison table.

  
![](https://files.readme.io/8df9bcf-image-carousel-example.png)

* * *

## 

Requirements and limitations

| Image| Full-card carousel image with custom button area  
---|---|---  
Supported file type| PNG, JPEG| PNG, JPEG, JPG  
Maximum image file size| 1 MB| 10 MB  
Image aspect ratio| Must be 1:1| Must be greater than 1:3  
Maximum number of cards per message| 10 cards| 10 cards  
  
* * *

## 

Compare image carousel message and carousel message

The following table summarizes the differences between the image carousel message type and the [carousel message type](doc:message-type-carousel).

| Image carousel message| Carousel message  
---|---|---  
Supported channels| LINE is the only supported channel.| • Messenger   
• Instagram   
• LINE   
• WebChat  
Image| Images can't be configured with a link.| Images can be configured with a link.   
When users click the image, they'll be sent to the app or web page specified by the link.  
Buttons| Each carousel card can contain one button.| Each carousel card can contain up to three buttons.  
Title and description| Carousel cards don't have titles or descriptions.| Each carousel card must have a title and description.  
Usage| Since image carousel messages are relatively simple, this message type is suitable for displaying a product or service catalog, e.g. displaying featured or new products.| Since carousel messages can contain more content and multiple interactive elements (users can click the image or the buttons), this message type is suitable for:   
• Communicating information   
• Allowing users to navigate multiple options  
  
* * *

## 

Create an image carousel

Click **Add module** , then select **Image carousel**. There are two options for image carousel types; refer to the following sections for setup instructions:

  * **Images** : Each card can contain a single [button](doc:button).
  * **Full-card carousel image with custom button area**: Fully customize the interactive layout of the card by adding action regions. Each card can have multiple action regions, and each action region can be configured with on-click actions and triggered actions.

![](https://files.readme.io/6b2bea5-add-image-carousel.png)

### 

Images

Upload your image, then click on the button to configure its settings, such as the on-click action and optional [triggered actions](doc:triggered-actions).

![](https://files.readme.io/5082424-image-carousel-button.png)

### 

Full-card carousel image with custom button area

  1. Upload your image, then click the three horizontal dots and select **Set action** to set up the image's interactive layout.

![](https://files.readme.io/1f1cf3c-image-carousel-set-action.png)

  2. Click and drag with your mouse to create an interactive region on the image. You can add multiple interactive regions in each image. After you're done, click **Confirm**.

![](https://files.readme.io/7adcc03-image-carousel-custom-layout.png)

  3. Configure on-click actions for each interactive region. Click on the interactive region, then configure settings such as its name, the on-click action, and optionally, [triggered actions](doc:triggered-actions). Click **Done** to save your settings. Repeat this step for every interactive region you created.

![](https://files.readme.io/e1e6b9a-image-carousel-on-click-action.png)

* * *

## 

Manage image carousel cards

### 

Add a card

To add a carousel card, click the **+** icon to the right of the last card of the carousel.

![](https://files.readme.io/d93c498-image-carousel-add.gif)

After adding multiple cards, you'll be able to navigate to the edit screens of each carousel card by clicking the arrows on the left and right of the settings window.

![](https://files.readme.io/63ad9de-image-carousel-navigate.gif)

### 

Change card sequence

To change the sequence of a carousel card, click and drag the colored box below the card. The gray boxes represent the other cards in the carousel—use these as a reference to reposition the current card (the colored box) into the desired position in the sequence.

![](https://files.readme.io/5a4437d-image-carousel-reposition.gif)

### 

Delete a card or the carousel

To delete a single carousel card or the entire carousel message, click the delete icon and select one of the following options:

  * **Delete this card** : Delete the current carousel card.
  * **Delete this carousel** : Delete the entire carousel message (all cards will be deleted).

![](https://files.readme.io/22b265d-image-carousel-delete.gif)

> 🚧
> 
> ### 
> 
> Caution
> 
>   * Deleted cards can't be recovered.
>   * If you delete a carousel, the settings for the cards in the carousel can't be recovered.
>

# Imagemap

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Supported channels
> 
>   * LINE
> 


Imagemap messages let users interact with tappable areas on full size images. With this message type, you can set tappable areas to open links, trigger actions, or track engagement with tags.

* * *

## 

Compare imagemap and image messages

The following table summarizes the differences between the imagemap and [image messages](doc:message-type-image). Use the table below to compare message types and choose the best fit for your campaign.

Feature| Imagemap messages| Image messages  
---|---|---  
Supported channels| LINE is the only supported channel.| Available for all channels.  
Image| Create customizable tappable areas in an image to open URLs, navigate to bot modules, or trigger actions.| Single static image without interactive elements.  
Usage| For campaigns that need interaction with specific areas of a single image.| Share simple visual content when no interaction is needed.  
  
* * *

## 

Create an imagemap message

Follow these steps to create an imagemap message:

  1. Go to **Flow** > **Add module** and select **Imagemap** from the list.

![](https://files.readme.io/02129be5e6b8009c999376d3c71cdedd86de47788346981eac5d16ab21362c73-Imagemap.png)

  2. Select an aspect ratio. Click **...** > **Change image** in the upper-right corner to upload an image file no larger than 1 MB.

![](https://files.readme.io/9a6e98c512c2720e597cc1b83719d88eb9d23418757c1f632bb3aa33f716e974-Aspect-ratio.png)

  3. Select **Edit layout** and set up the imagemap layout based on your aspect ratio.



Aspect ratio| Description  
---|---  
1:1, 1:2| Choose an imagemap layout that divides your image into predefined tappable areas.![](https://files.readme.io/d864b50a448716dbe62c11f541df944307b1a8038947b607ab44c33c93c30ed5-ratio-layout.png)  
Custom| Manually drag to define tappable areas and assign actions.![](https://files.readme.io/edf194361bc30a4f0f0426f93e4ba3d250d52398361b830ba25e0021bb1e2230-custom_layout.png)  
  
  4. Hover over a tappable area and select an action:


  * **None** : Leave this area without an action.
  * **Go-to Module** : Direct users to a specific chatbot module.
  * **Open a URL** : Enter a link to take users to a webpage.

![](https://files.readme.io/3657eaadec23c11700b47a198f4d43926eac9cf394e9c170fe97a04549c2e040-Tappable-action.png)

  5. Add a descriptive **Alt text** to explain the imagemap's purpose in LINE notifications.

![](https://files.readme.io/eb41e9a6928a384dca65c1cb86724d189f41a1b5ce7d44433b59bb83d5d64466-alt-text.png)

* * *

## 

Best practices

  * Use **[Triggered actions](doc:triggered-actions)** to tag users for retargeting in future remarketing campaigns.
  * Test your imagemap to ensure tappable areas align seamlessly for easy interaction.

# JSON

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Supported channels
> 
>   * Instagram
>   * Messenger
>   * LINE
> 


JSON messages enable external API calls from BotBonnie flows via webhook URL. Use them to reply with real-time data from outside the platform, such as user information or custom content from your systems, for creating dynamic flows and personalized responses.

* * *

## 

Prerequisites

Before using JSON messages, make sure you have:

  * A webhook URL that returns a valid response.
  * An endpoint on your server that can handle webhook requests from BotBonnie.



For technical details, see the [JSON messaging reference](doc:message-type-json-reference).

* * *

## 

Add JSON message

To add JSON message to your flow:

  1. Go to **Flow** > **Add module**.
  2. Select **JSON** as the message type to fetch data from your server.

![](https://files.readme.io/db2e51a6abcb98d45fe8f0bc5a5eaeaded4e2cb5717afc2b684ce6583af15d75-json-message-type.png)

  3. Enter your webhook URL, where BotBonnie will send a request to your server to retrieve data for your message. For example: `http://yourdormain.com/?fields=user,userParams`.
     * Include query parameters `?fields=user,userParams`) to pass additional user-specific data, such as user's email or custom parameters. See [requesting additional user data](doc:message-type-json-reference#requesting-additional-user-data) for a list of included information.
     * If your webhook URL doesn't include `?fields=user,userParams`, BotBonnie sends only the default user data.

![](https://files.readme.io/a31435454f9eb9d217b5e7235f7482a4d089aba38ac5fcc40f3b4dcb3673c726-json-url.png)

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> For JSON messages sent as direct replies, fees are typically not incurred directly from the messaging channel provider if sent within the channel's free reply window. This free reply window varies significantly by channel:
> 
>   * LINE: Around one minute.
>   * Instagram and Facebook Messenger: Usually around 24 hours.
> 

> 
> Messages sent after this free reply window closes may incur charges directly from the messaging channel provider, regardless of message type.

To learn more about request structure, supported formats, and signature verification, see the [JSON message reference](doc:message-type-json-reference).

# JSON Message Reference

Integrate the JSON message type with your external systems via webhook URLs. This lets you dynamically generate messages, exchange data, and automate interactions based on user actions within your flows.

The following sections provide the technical specifications for JSON message integration:

  * How JSON message delivery works
  * Setting up a webhook endpoint
  * Request headers
  * Request body
  * Response format



* * *

## 

How JSON message delivery works

When a user needs dynamic, real-time information from your BotBonnie flow (for example, checking their loyalty points), BotBonnie seamlessly fetches a tailored response from your server and delivers it back to the user.

  1. User triggers action: The user interacts with your BotBonnie flow to trigger a request for dynamic content. The trigger's action could be by tapping a specific button, sending a particular keyword, or triggering a predefined event within the conversation.
  2. Webhook request sent: BotBonnie sends a POST request to your configured webhook endpoint, containing the user's identifier and context data.
  3. Server processes request: Your webhook server receives the request, queries your database for the user's information, and returns a supported JSON message format in the HTTP response (for example, a text message like "You have 1,250 points!").
  4. Message delivered: BotBonnie processes your server's JSON response and delivers the dynamically-generated message back to the user.

![](https://files.readme.io/d9ae9ee926589e7dc7d45a1bbe5091c8609aba6be84cc64845b262960866ecc8-webhook_flow_diagram_2.png)

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> Make sure your server responds within three seconds to ensure successful message delivery.

* * *

## 

Set up a webhook endpoint

To integrate [JSON messages](doc:message-type-json) into your BotBonnie flows, configure a webhook endpoint on your server. The webhook URL you enter in the BotBonnie flow must be configured to:

  * Parse the request body for bot and user details.
  * Respond within the expected timeframe to prevent webhook delivery failures.
  * Return one of the supported JSON message formats.



* * *

## 

Request headers

BotBonnie sends requests that include the following headers:

Header name| Description  
---|---  
`X-Hub-Signature`| Verifies that the request originated from BotBonnie using a hash.  
`Content-Type`| Specifies that the request body is in JSON format (`application/json`).  
  
> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Security best practice
> 
> Always verify the `X-Hub-Signature` header in incoming API requests using your API secret for authentication.
> 
> Go to **Settings** > **Advanced** , then click the copy icon next to **API Secret**.
> 
> ![](https://files.readme.io/fab504cfaad4f6e75002d33053083c2c2fa58e56c8754e86093abb6c9773212b-get-api-secret.png)

The following example shows how to validate the signature using your API secret:

JavaScript
    
    
    const crypto = require("crypto"); 
    function getSignature(buf) {
      const hmac = crypto.createHmac("sha1", process.env.BOTBONNIE_API_SECRET);
      hmac.update(buf, "utf-8");
      return "sha1=" + hmac.digest("hex");
    }
    
    function verifyRequest(req, res, buf) {
      const expected = req.headers['x-hub-signature'];
      const calculated = getSignature(buf);
      if (expected !== calculated) {
        throw new Error("Invalid signature.");
      }
    }

* * *

## 

Request body

The request body includes the following basic bot and environment information sent to your webhook endpoint by default:

Name| Type| Description  
---|---|---  
`bot_id`| string| The unique identifier for the bot.  
`bot_uid`| string| The encrypted user ID. (Deprecated)  
`bot_raw_uid`| string| The unencrypted user ID for user identification.  
`bot_pid`| string| The Facebook page ID.  
`bot_channel`| integer| The channel ID:   
• `0`: Facebook Messenger  
• `1`: LINE   
• `2`: WebChat   
• `5`: Instagram   
• `6`: WhatsApp  
`env`| string| The environment the bot is running in (for example, `prod` for production).  
  
### 

Request additional user data

To receive additional user-specific data, add `?fields=user,userParams` to your webhook URL.

When you include these query parameters in the webhook URL for a [JSON message](doc:message-type-json), the request body expands to include user profile data, custom parameters, and demographic information, in addition to the default bot and environment details.

Name| Type| Description  
---|---|---  
`user`| object| This object contains the following core user profile data provided by the messaging channel:   
• `pic` (profile picture URL)   
• `statusMessage` (user's profile status message)   
• `rawId` (user ID)   
• `name` (username)   
• `created` (timestamp)  
`userParams`| object| This object contains custom user parameters saved in BotBonnie. See[Custom parameters](doc:user-properties#custom-parameters)for more details.  
`userDemographics`| object| This object contains the following user demographic attributes:   
• `email` (Email)   
• `birthday` (Birthday)   
• `gender` (Gender)   
• `phone` (Phone number)  
• `location`(Location)   
  
This object is included automatically when both the `user` and `userParams` objects are requested together via the `fields` query parameter (that is, `?fields=user,userParams`). See[User demographics](doc:user-properties#user-demographics)for more details.  
  
Below is an example of a JSON request body with default data and additional user data:

JSON
    
    
    {
      "bot_id": "bot-123456789",
      "bot_raw_uid": "Ubcd000000ab000000c00de000f000g0",
      "bot_pid": "a00b00cd00000e0ab00000cde00a0abcdef0000000000a00bcd00000ab0000cd00",
      "bot_channel": 1,
      "env": "prod",
      "user": {
        "pic": "https://example.com/image.jpg",
        "statusMessage": "hello ",
        "rawId": "Ubcd000000ab000000c00de000f000g0",
        "created": 1234567890123,
        "name": "Jane"
      },
      "userParams": {
        "input": null
      },
      "userDemographics": {
        "email": 
          "value": "",
          "timestamp": 1234567890123
        }
      }
    }

* * *

## 

Response format

Your webhook must return a JSON response with one of the supported message formats. The structure of the response determines how the message is displayed to the user and ensures it is delivered correctly after they triggered the JSON message.

### 

Supported JSON message formats

The JSON message type supports various formats for delivering dynamic content:

Message type| Specifications| Description| Quick reply| Button  
---|---|---|---|---  
Carousel| –| A scrollable collection of items, each containing an image, description, and one or more buttons.| Supported| Supported  
Image| • JPG   
• PNG   
• GIF| Sends a single image.| Supported| Not supported  
Text| Up to 320 characters.| Sends a text message.| Supported| Supported  
Video| • MP4| Sends a video file.| Supported| Not supported  
Audio| • MP3| Sends an audio file.| Supported| Not supported  
Media| Facebook URLs only.| Sends media from a Facebook source.| Supported| Supported  
  
#### 

Carousel message

Send a scrollable horizontal carousel of multiple cards. Each card can feature a title, subtitle, an image, and up to three buttons.

JSON
    
    
    {
      "type": "gallery",
      "cards": [
        {
          "title": "title1",
          "subtitle": "subtitle1",
          "imageUrl": "https://example.com/image.jpg"
        },
        {
          "title": "title2",
          "subtitle": "subtitle2",
          "imageUrl": "https://example.com/image.jpg",
          "buttons": [
            {
              "title": "button1",
              "type": "postback",
              "value": "module-MODULE_ID"
            },
            {
              "title": "button2",
              "type": "web_url",
              "value": "http://url.com"
            }
          ]
        }
      ],
    "style": "image_carousel",
    "text": "string"
    }

#### 

Image message

Send a single image message along with optional quick replies.

JSON
    
    
    {
      "type": "image",
      "imageUrl": "https://example.com/image.jpg",
      "quickReplies": [
        {
          "payload": "module-MODULE_ID",
          "title": "Quick reply 1",
          "type": "text"
        }
      ]
    }

#### 

Text message

Send plain text message along with optional buttons and quick replies.

JSON
    
    
    {
      "type": "text",
      "text": "description",
      "buttons": [
        {
          "title": "button1",
          "type": "postback",
          "value": "module-MODULE_ID"
        },
        {
          "title": "button2",
          "type": "web_url",
          "value": "http://url.com"
        }
      ],
      "quickReplies": [
        {
          "title": "quickreply",
          "type": "text",
          "payload": "module-MODULE_ID"
        }
      ]
    }

#### 

Video message

Send a video message along with optional quick replies.

JSON
    
    
    {
      "type": "video",
      "videoUrl": "https://example.com/video.mp4",
      "quickReplies": [
        {
          "payload": "module-MODULE_ID",
          "title": "Quick reply 1",
          "type": "text"
        }
      ]
    }

#### 

Audio message

Send an audio message along with optional quick replies.

JSON
    
    
    {
      "type": "audio",
      "audioUrl": "https://example.com/audio.mp3",
      "quickReplies": [
        {
          "payload": "module-MODULE_ID",
          "title": "Quick reply 1",
          "type": "text"
        }
      ]
    }

#### 

Media: Facebook URL

Send a media URL supports both images and videos hosted on Facebook along with optional buttons.

JSON
    
    
    {
      "type": "media",
      "mediaType": "<image|video>",
      "url": "<FACEBOOK_URL>",
      "buttons": [
        {
          "title": "button1",
          "type": "postback",
          "value": "module-MODULE_ID"
        }
      ]
    }

# One-Time Notification Request

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Supported channels
> 
>   * Messenger
>   * LINE
> 


A one-time notification request allows you to ask users for permission to send them another message within one year of their acceptance. Users can accept the request by clicking **Notify Me** , and you'll have the opportunity to send one notification to those users via broadcast. One-time notifications allow you to send the notification without being restricted to the 24 hour messaging window.

The one-time notification workflow consists of three steps:

  1. Send the one-time notification request.
  2. Users click **Notify me** to accept the request.
  3. Send a broadcast within one year to reach the users who opted in.

![](https://files.readme.io/e076bed-one-time-notification-request-flow.png)

* * *

## 

Send one-time notifications

Follow the steps below to send a one-time notification to your audience:

  1. (Facebook Pages only) Request access to one-time notification for your Facebook Page
  2. Select a method for sending the request: Choose whether the request via chatbot flows, broadcasts, or Growth Tools.
  3. Create the request message: Configure the settings for your one-time notification request, such as the image and title.
  4. Broadcast the one-time notification: Use broadcasts to send the one-time notification the interval you specified in the initial request.



### 

1\. (Facebook Pages only) Request access to one-time notification for your Facebook Page

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> If your chatbot isn't connected to a Facebook page, skip this step and go to step 2 directly.

  1. In the settings page for your Facebook Page, go to **New Pages Experience > Advanced Messaging**.
  2. Find the section titled **Requested features**.
  3. Submit a request to use **One-time notification**.

![](https://files.readme.io/596fea5-request-feature.png)

### 

2\. Select a method for sending the one-time notification request

Select one of the following methods to send the one-time notifications request to your audience:

  * Chatbot flows
  * Broadcasts
  * Growth Tools



#### 

Send request via chatbot flows

In your chatbot flows, click **Add module > One-time notification request**, then create the request message.

![](https://files.readme.io/e2847ee84c615cb15323face87d64de6f24a137f3755f38feaee7a7d7efbe453-flow-one-time.png)

#### 

Sending request via broadcast

Go to **Broadcasting > Broadcasts**, then click **+Broadcast** to create a broadcast with a module containing the one-time notification request message, then create the request message

![](https://files.readme.io/46609c8c863458d4bc4e6fed09696a4308a0855b6221189664c5f213fa920fde-one-time-broadcast.png)

#### 

Sending request via Growth Tools

Go to **Growth Tools** , click **+Growth Tool** , select one of the following supported options, then create the request message.

Channel| Supported options  
---|---  
Facebook| • **Start Conversation with Short Link**   
• **Facebook Auto reply for post comments** (Private message reply)   
• **Facebook Multiple Auto Reply** (Private message reply)   
  
LINE| • **Start Conversation with Short Link**   
• **LINE Smart Link**  
  
![](https://files.readme.io/14492cdd334a31c1e38ea9c0100f13f704d2f3d60e469124b65971aeeffff4a8-one-time-growth-tool.png)

### 

3\. Create the one-time notification request message

  1. In the input box under **Get notified** , input a message describing what type of notification the user will receive in the future. This message is limited to 65 characters.

![](https://files.readme.io/df17dfd4db536d0c6cc922f43ba96d5c8d0723c1ab8d7b2fa44bee1b4cf26863-module-one-time.png)

  2. Configure button settings for the **Notify Me** button. Under **On click** , select the module that should be sent to the user after clicking **Notify Me** to indicate that they've opted in to the one-time notification.

![](https://files.readme.io/f5b6cca64bc5a84d74c14c21e3fb2af6ea6a791e793eee3c069a249f96848ef1-one-time-on-click.png)

All users who accept the request will be added to the one-time notification user list called **Default notification list**. In the next step, you'll be able to send a broadcast to all users in **Default notification list**.

### 

4\. Broadcast the one-time notification

Once you're ready to send the message, use a broadcast to notify the users who opted in to the one-time notification request.

  1. In the left menu, go to **Broadcasting > Broadcasts**, then click **\+ Create broadcast**.

![](https://files.readme.io/b649cb3fb83a186af8634633c4df4b957046b078e59b9265ec0502ec77dbc2da-create-a-broadcast.png)

  2. Under **Filter user segments** , click **+Add condition** , then select **One-time notification list** from the dropdown menu. Select when to broadcast the message, then click **Next**.

![](https://files.readme.io/671e7ad-broadcast-settings.png)

  3. Select an existing module or create a new module that will be sent in the broadcast, then click **Next**.



> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> You can only send a module containing a single message. Modules containing multiple messages can't be used in one-time notification broadcasts.

![](https://files.readme.io/892ae1d-broadcast-select-module.png)

  4. Click **Broadcast message** to send the broadcast. Users in the default one-time notification list, i.e. users who accepted the one-time notification request, will receive the module you selected for this broadcast.



> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Broadcast reports
> 
> A [broadcast report](doc:broadcasting-broadcast-reports) containing performance data will be available several minutes after the broadcast is sent.

# Recurring Notifications Request

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Supported channels
> 
>   * Messenger
> 


Facebook Messenger's recurring notifications feature enables you to proactively message your users at a predefined cadence, driving re-engagement and creating a customer-centric experience. With recurring notifications, you can send notifications without being restricted to the 24-hour messaging window, enabling you to stay connected with users throughout the customer journey.

Recurring notifications are great for marketing scenarios such as:

  * New product launches and restocks
  * Countdowns and early-bird discounts for upcoming sales
  * Event promotions



The workflow for using recurring notifications consists of three steps:

  1. Send a request for sending recurring notifications at a certain frequency.
  2. User opts in to your recurring notifications.
  3. Send broadcasts at the previously selected frequency to users who opted in.

![](https://files.readme.io/5e75a2b-recurring-notifications-flow.png)

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
>   * To enable this feature, please reach out to your customer success manager.
>   * See Facebook's official documentation for [best practices](https://developers.facebook.com/docs/messenger-platform/send-messages/recurring-notifications#best-practices) and [limitations](https://developers.facebook.com/docs/messenger-platform/send-messages/recurring-notifications#limitations).
> 


* * *

## 

Create a recurring notification

Follow the steps below to start send recurring notifications to your audience:

  1. Select a method for sending the request: Choose whether the request via chatbot flows, broadcasts, or Growth Tools.
  2. Create the request message: Configure the settings for your recurring notification request, such as the image and title.
  3. Broadcast the recurring notifications: Use broadcasts to send the recurring notification the interval you specified in the initial request.



### 

1\. Select a method for sending the recurring notifications request

Select one of the following methods to send the recurring notifications request to your audience:

  * Chatbot flows
  * Broadcasts
  * Growth tools



After you've selected a method for sending the request, create the request message.

#### 

Send request via chatbot flows

In your chatbot flows, click **Add module** > **Recurring notification request** , then create the request message.

![](https://files.readme.io/22a504ad99e7221ef25a7739e3d5a8dbf94d5d20107ad0c63ad4e5ffd6685371-module-flow-recurring.png)

#### 

Send request via broadcast

Go to **Broadcasting** , click **+Create broadcast** , add a module that contains the recurring notifications request, then create the request message.

![](https://files.readme.io/9027fe07b892910a9743851aef671864e76bc97133cad5dbf6f86298d7233585-recurring-broadcast.png)

#### 

Send request via Growth tools

Go to **Growth tools** , click **\+ Add growth tool** , select one of the supported options, add a private reply module that contains the recurring notifications request, then create the request message.

Platform| Supported options  
---|---  
Facebook| • **Facebook Auto reply for post comment** (Private message reply)   
• **Facebook Multiple Auto Reply** (Private message reply)  
  
![](https://files.readme.io/58061a011efe80ec99eb3dd2d432421819858db71f2afa6a96006816336c27da-recurring-growth-tool.png)

### 

2\. Create the recurring notifications request message

Configure the following settings for your recurring notifications request:

Setting| Description  
---|---  
Requested broadcast frequency| Select the frequency of your recurring notifications:   
• Daily message for 6 months.   
![](https://files.readme.io/199cf65812bc0bc67370d9a97a9f2651fbd452eb8a348322ed4728e1e91077c2-daily-message-frequency.png)  
Image| This image is displayed at the top of your recurring notifications request. The image file must meet the following requirements:   
• File format must be GIF, JPG, or PNG   
• File size must be less than 1 MB   
![](https://files.readme.io/3f87288-recurring-notification-request-image.png)  
Notification title| This title should describe what the future notifications will be about.![](https://files.readme.io/efd5cd4-recurring-notification-request-message.png)  
Opt-in button| Select the module that will be sent to users when they click the opt-in button to indicate that they've opted in to recurring notifications.   
Users who click this button will be added to the default recurring notifications user list. You'll be able to use this list to send broadcasts to all users who opted in.   
![](https://files.readme.io/f702d69-recurring-notification-request-button-settings.png)  
  
### 

3\. Broadcast the recurring notifications

Once you're ready to send a follow-up notification, use a broadcast to notify the users who opted in to the recurring notifications request. You'll be able to send broadcasts at the cadence you selected for the **Requested broadcast frequency** in your module settings.

  1. In the left-hand menu, go to **Broadcasting** , then click **\+ Create broadcast**.

![](https://files.readme.io/72b605834ba0f2500db2a1ed2425357e334f06758f65fdbf334267e612dfcaa0-create-a-broadcast.png)

  2. Under **Filter user segments** , click **+Add condition** , then select **Recurring notification** from the dropdown menu. Select when to broadcast the message, then click **Next**.

![](https://files.readme.io/4ecc69d-recurring-notifications-broadcast-settings.png)

  3. Select an existing module or create a new module that will be sent in the broadcast, then click **Next**.



> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> You can only send a module containing a single message. Modules containing multiple messages can't be used in broadcasts for recurring notifications.

![](https://files.readme.io/08dc98e-recurring-notifications-broadcast-message.png)

  4. Click **Broadcast message** to send the broadcast. Users in the default recurring notification list, i.e. users who accepted the recurring notifications request, will receive the module you selected for this broadcast.



> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Broadcast reports
> 
> A [broadcast report](doc:broadcasting-broadcast-reports) containing performance data will be available several minutes after the broadcast is sent.

__Updated 7 months ago

# Text

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Supported channels
> 
> Text messages are available for all messaging channels.

Text messages are messages that only contain text content. This message type can be treated like an SMS message.

![](https://files.readme.io/ea0cef3-text-message.png)

* * *

## 

Requirements and limitations

Text messages are limited to 640 characters.

* * *

## 

Usage tips

  * Personalize the message by [including the user's name](doc:modules-and-messages#personalizing-messages) in the message.
  * Consider the screen size of mobile devices and limit message content accordingly. Simple and concise messages are easier to understand and may increase the user’s willingness to read.
  * If you want to send large amounts of text, you can split the content and send it across multiple messages.

# Video

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Supported channels
> 
>   * Messenger
>   * Instagram
>   * LINE
>   * WebChat
>   * WhatsApp
> 


You can send video messages by uploading a video file.

![](https://files.readme.io/b996de8bc756d25aef78ea4131e8ff3e2c67f2b934bfe34bf3ede1da8278e57a-video-message.png)

* * *

## 

Create a video message

Follow these steps to create a video message:

  1. In **Flow** , click **Add module** , then select **Video**.

![](https://files.readme.io/3e1a75c88a00013d466d7feca94a6ef26439e7fbf8b25668b3c6143b5928d171-add-video.png)

  2. Click the **Upload video here** area. Ensure your video file meets the following requirements:
     * Maximum file size: 200 MB
     * Supported format: MP4

![](https://files.readme.io/4cb367b1532e51357a8aba8a587074a00783733644b2c14f23bb1e253feed1d5-upload-video.png)

  3. (Optional) Add or change a custom cover image for the video message:
     1. Click the three horizontal dots in the upper-right corner of your video file.
     2. Click the **Change to customized cover image** to upload. Ensure your cover image meets the requirements.

![](https://files.readme.io/f039cf61bb650cc46a310885808a9d4829b520bbc41f2e0df99a51b080f62245-video-customized-cover-image.png)

### 

Custom cover image

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Supported channels
> 
>   * LINE
> 


Custom cover images are only displayed in LINE video messages. When a custom cover image is applied, its aspect ratio determines how the video is displayed. To ensure correct display, upload a cover image that exactly matches your video’s aspect ratio and meets the following requirements:

  * Maximum file size: 1 MB or less
  * Supported formats: PNG, JPEG, or JPG



If a custom cover image isn't provided, or for video messages sent on channels other than LINE, the system automatically uses a screenshot taken at the video's 1-second mark as the cover. This default cover maintains the video's original aspect ratio.

__Updated 7 months ago

* * *

# Video Imagemap

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Supported channels
> 
>   * LINE
> 


Video imagemap messages allow you to upload a video file that plays in full-screen mode when a user taps the message. This message type includes a single button that appears in full-screen mode, which links to a URL you set in the message settings.

* * *

## 

Video imagemap message vs video message

The following table summarizes the differences between the video imagemap message type and the [video message type](doc:message-type-video).

| Video imagemap message| Video message  
---|---|---  
Supported channels| LINE is the only supported channel.| • Facebook   
• Instagram   
• LINE   
• WebChat   
• WhatsApp  
Buttons (with links)| Video imagemap messages can include one button, which is visible in full screen viewing mode.| Video messages can’t contain buttons.  
File size| Video files can be up to 200 MB| Video files can be up to 200 MB  
Appearance| Full message width:   
  
![](https://files.readme.io/9118240-video-imagemap-full-width.png)| Standard message width:   
  
![](https://files.readme.io/206ec30-video-imagemap-standard-width.png)  
  
* * *

## 

Create a video imagemap message

Follow these steps to create a video imagemap message:

  1. In **Flow** , click **Add module** , then select **Video imagemap**.

![](https://files.readme.io/ef883f3b6269fc9e32cb1266fc8a449e2ecf33729d4352ff48ac7cd438b2312e-video-imagemap.png)

  2. Click the **Upload video here** area to upload a video. The video file must meets the following requirements:
     * Maximum file size: 200 MB
     * Supported format: MP4
  3. Click **Learn More** to edit your button's name and enter a URL for the **On click** setting. Note that this setting is fixed to **Open URL** and can't be changed.

![](https://files.readme.io/8224f163b1ce91105fee1ac59611dfbd9f4cb08790d27a401dc16e69db33d0b0-button-setting-video-imagemap.png)

  4. Add a descriptive **Alt text** to explain the video imagemap's purpose in LINE notifications.

![](https://files.readme.io/623b27825fcf28d838f14367940fb9d2590ac4c5f91c1ac0c2072cfd81a22eef-alt-text-video-imagemap.png)

__Updated 6 months ago

# MGM

A referral program can encourage your existing users to tell their friends and families about your business. Using BotBonnie's member-get-member (MGM) kit, you can:

  * Reward users who successfully invite their friends to interact with your brand
  * Set the number of referrals users have to reach to get rewards
  * See the number of participants and successful referrals in the performance report



### 

How it works

Here's how an MGM campaign works:

  1. User A shares an invitation link either through LINE messages or a Facebook post.
  2. User B clicks on the invitation link shared by User A and is directed to your LINE OA or the chat page of your Facebook Page. A welcome message is shown to User B. User B can now share the campaign with friends.
  3. User A gets notified for successfully inviting User B. Different notifications can be sent based on whether User A has met the referral target.

![](https://files.readme.io/f782bb7-MGM_diagram.png)

* * *

## 

MGM kit setup

### 

1\. Add the kit to your flow

From the left menu, go to **Flow** , then click **Advanced kits** and select **MGM**.

![](https://files.readme.io/f7861d4832069064f1b6acbebdb94689d8ce8076c73fdfdef5aef4afa80ec308-menu-mgm.png)

Click **Start editing** or double-click the kit to enter the sub-flow of the kit.

![](https://files.readme.io/0813f95-mgm-start-editing.png)

> 👍
> 
> When setting up an MGM kit for the first time, a Quick Setup will show up to guide you through the basic settings.

Click the **Invite friends** module.

![](https://files.readme.io/ccbbe73-mgm-invite-friends.png)

### 

2\. Configure basic kit settings

Under the **Basic** section, complete the following settings.

  * **Kit name** : Enter a kit name that helps you identify the kit on the console.
  * **Set campaign period** : To set a campaign period, switch on the toggle and set the start time and the end time.
  * **Out-of-period module** : Select the module you want to display if the user enters the kit outside the campaign period.

![](https://files.readme.io/6e2afc89c2e8c47d78993f2b252691f21be7a634b8e3c98dd4463968604e99b4-mgm-basic.png)

### 

3\. Set the ways to share invitations

You can let users share the referral campaign with their friends through LINE messages or Facebook posts. Use the **Share via LINE messages** and **Share via Facebook posts** toggles to enable the channels you want to use.

![](https://files.readme.io/dfcd82925abb4e7f71a0ce74065b694611b314be3ae9e55e164a6f634322bf7c-mgm-sharing-options.png)

Enter the **Button name** of the share button that will be shown in the start module for the inviter.

  * After clicking the share button for LINE, the participant can choose to share the message to friends, groups, or chats.
  * After clicking the share button for Facebook, the participant is directed to the **Share on Facebook** page on Facebook with the default invitation message.

![](https://files.readme.io/d6f375f-ShareButton_Click.png)

Under **Default message for inviting friends** or **Default post content for inviting friends** section, create the default content of the LINE message or Facebook post that will be shown after clicking the share button. Keep the default message within 78 characters to ensure readability.

The content can still be edited before being shared on Facebook or shared with a single friend, chat, or group via LINE.

![](https://files.readme.io/ee54c0f8bb1a6161250575ebf9b2dcc124caedfbc1d95b15679133d5dcae363e-mgm-sharingoptions.png)

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> **Sharing via LINE messages** is only supported in mobile devices.
> 
>   * LINE invitation messages might not work properly if the message exceeds 1000 bytes. To avoid this issue, login to LINE Developers console, click the LINE Login channel, go to the **LIFF** tab, and enable the **shareTargetPicker** option. Alternatively, keep the message short to avoid this issue.
> 
![](https://files.readme.io/c89197a-Screen_Shot_2024-01-25_at_11.03.08_AM.png)
> 
>   * When the default invitation message for LINE includes unicode characters or emojis, the number of characters allowed is much lower than the character count shown on the console. The invitation message will be truncated if the character limit is exceeded.
> 


### 

4\. Set the referral target

If you want to reward inviters only after they have successfully referred multiple friends, enable the **Set the referral target** option and select the target number of referrals.

![](https://files.readme.io/4e91559-Referral_target.png)

When the referral target is enabled, different notifications can be sent based on whether the inviter has met the target. When disabled, the inviter receives the same message module for each successful referral.

![](https://files.readme.io/68995e7-Screen_Shot_2023-06-12_at_2.32.52_PM.png)

When **Only count referrals when the invitee is a new user** is selected, the invited user needs to be a new user who does not exist in the user list of your bot in order to count toward the referral target.

New users are defined as:

  * LINE users who are not already a follower of your LINE Official Account. Users who blocked your account and then unblocked do not count as new users.
  * Facebook users who have never messaged your Facebook Page before (via Messenger).



### 

5\. Select the welcome message for invitees

Select the start module you want to display to the invitee when they click the invitation link to join the campaign.

![](https://files.readme.io/1e048f9835ff8d1d46750a15000c5a59012c4f0f794ea41279c5f6bd0337be96-mgm-welcomemessage.png)

### 

6\. Adjust referral rankings

The **Show rankings** toggle allows you to show a ranking of participants by the number of referrals. You can change the **Button name** of the **View rankings** button shown in the start module for the inviter.

Next to **Display rank position** , select the number of ranks to display (for example, top 10 participants).

![](https://files.readme.io/bfb5b65f1d8599a3bf6c794aa34b5e5694661d7bc0b6ca8554e1dfa9bc7537b2-mgm-rankings.png)

### 

7\. Check the messaging platform status

In the **Others** section, check the status of your messaging platforms and make sure everything is set up properly.

![](https://files.readme.io/b70db3e83092cdfa3c56574d3255094e864e39f01756d00dc1c597d121a1bd11-mgm-others.png)

### 

8\. Adjust the Flows modules and add rewards

Go back to the Flows page, and adjust the content of each module to suit your campaign.

To grant rewards to participants who made successful referrals, you can add a button in the "success" notification module and set the **On click** action to an advanced kit as such a lucky wheel, scratch-off, or prize distribution kit.

![](https://files.readme.io/a8359d4-GetReward.png)

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Best practice
> 
> Test the kit before launching your campaign to ensure everything works as expected.

* * *

## 

Reset kit data

Resetting a kit is useful if you ran the campaign to do internal testing, and now want to clear all test data before officially launching the campaign.

Resetting a kit will clear all data associated with the kit.

  * All records of participant data (for example, successful invitations) will be deleted
  * The ranking status will be cleared



> 🚧
> 
> ### 
> 
> Caution
> 
> This action can't be reversed.

In the kit settings, go to the **Reset** section, then click **Reset**. You'll be prompted to type "Reset" into the input box, then click **Reset** again to confirm your choice.

![](https://files.readme.io/5f4f28eb5157824afd21041ae9bcceb38cdde1f1eee7f29e87d9eed85ce3cddf-mgm-reset.png)

* * *

## 

Performance report

To view the performance report of the MGM kit, go to **Analytics** > **Kit report** and click the view icon.

![](https://files.readme.io/7f061a3d1bfe9ae6dcbfc5d91c9c6d703a0a4351d7099a14e622abd4ed453d9f-mgm-viewperformace.png)

### 

Export the list of invited users

To export the list of invited users, click **Export list**.

![](https://files.readme.io/4972963-Screen_Shot_2023-06-12_at_1.55.15_PM.png)

Type the email address where you'd like the CSV file to be sent to, then click **Confirm**. A CSV file will be sent to you within an hour.

![](https://files.readme.io/0b7baa0-Screen_Shot_2023-06-12_at_2.02.00_PM.png)

The exported file contains a list of invited users with details such as the inviter's name, whether they are new users...etc.

![](https://files.readme.io/82d6e89-Screen_Shot_2023-06-12_at_2.05.18_PM.png)

### 

Performance summary

In the report, the following metrics are available.

  * **New users referred** : This is the number of referrals where the invited user is not already a follower of the LINE Official Account or has never had conversations with the Facebook Page.
  * **Old users reached:** This is the number of referrals where the invited user is already a follower of the LINE Official Account or has previously had conversations with the Facebook Page.
  * **Total inviters** : This is the number of users who made at least one successful referral.
  * **Average referrals per inviters** : This is the average number of referrals completed by users with at least one successful referral.

![](https://files.readme.io/e3914c3-Screen_Shot_2023-06-12_at_1.55.15_PM_copy.png)

### 

Detailed data

Select a time period to see the performance data during that period. There are four sections available:

  * Users successfully invited
  * Inviters with successful referrals
  * Distribution of successful referrals
  * Participant referral rankings

![](https://files.readme.io/d5bd988-Screen_Shot_2023-06-12_at_2.15.35_PM.png)
